1	---
2	name: roadmap-candidates
3	description: "Candidate next-feature directions for the voxel game — what's shipped vs still open (as of 2026-06-13)"
4	metadata: 
5	  node_type: memory
6	  type: project
7	  originSessionId: e2fc6b63-8357-4ed9-b4cc-94b55fc71192
8	---
9	
10	Highest-value next-feature directions for the voxel game, tracked across sessions. Shipped so far on `main`: an authentic-Minecraft UI pass, a juice & atmosphere pass (particles, night sky, transient weather), and **chests & persistent storage** (PR #16, merged — adds the game's first block-entity layer: `state.containers` keyed by voxel index, save format v4, a 27-slot chest, spill-on-break). All in CHANGELOG.
11	
12	**Released `v0.6.0`** (2026-06-13, tagged `v0.6.0`, package.json bumped) bundling the UI/juice/chests batch. Also opened **upstream PR davidhu0527/monecraft#6** (`hutusi:main` → `davidhu0527:main`, 32 commits) — the fork→upstream sync, david's to merge.
13	
14	**DECIDED next feature: Dungeons / loot structures**, to be planned in a fresh session (the user wants a clean context for the exploration + plan pass; target release **0.7.0**). World-reset is acceptable because it lands right after a release. When that session starts, run plan mode the way the chests feature was done (parallel Explore agents → Plan agent → ExitPlanMode), then implement in bisectable commit slices and PR to `hutusi/main`.
15	
16	Still open, in rough order of how strongly they now build on what exists:
17	
18	- **Dungeons / exploration & loot** ⭐ (agreed next) — a worldgen structure pass placing rooms with **pre-filled loot chests** (reuses the just-built container/block-entity layer + the `placeStructures` hook in `generation.ts` + the `rollMobDrops` loot pattern) and optionally a mob spawner. Fills the "no exploration payoff" gap and gives chests a reason to exist beyond storage. Minimal first slice: a few small underground cobble rooms with 1–2 tiered loot chests each, optional drip-spawner. Cost: worldgen change → **`SAVE_KEY` bump + worldgen hash re-baseline** (documented policy in docs/testing.md). Medium–high effort.
19	- **Caves & survival tension** — torches + a real block-light propagation system (caves are currently lit like the surface, no darkness), plus lava + lava/drowning damage + an oxygen meter. Largest/riskiest: touches world storage (a per-voxel light channel) and the mesher hot path; est. ~1,500–2,200 LoC, multi-week.
20	- **Endgame & ranged combat** — bows/arrows as a transient projectile system (skeletons shoot back) + a boss encounter that makes the diamond grind a goal. Smallest/lowest-risk (~550–700 LoC, a few days), self-contained, **no save/worldgen impact**.
21	
22	Other gaps still noted: no block light, no fluid hazard, no ranged combat, no endgame target. The game already has caves, ores, ~120 houses, 5 (cosmetic) biomes, static water, 6 mobs, farming/breeding/cooking/beds, 7-tier gear, and now chests. See [[review-fixes-no-push]] and [[no-pr-attribution]] for the user's workflow preferences.
23

1	# Gameplay Reference
2	
3	Scannable tables for everything in the game: recipes, blocks, mobs, and item
4	stats. For how it all fits together, read the [player manual](manual.md).
5	
6	> **The code is the source of truth.** These tables are a convenience snapshot of
7	> `lib/game/recipes.ts`, `lib/game/items.ts`, `lib/world/blocks.ts`,
8	> `lib/game/mobs.ts`, and `lib/game/mobLoot.ts`. If a number here disagrees with
9	> the game, the code wins — and this page needs an update.
10	
11	## Recipes
12	
13	**32 recipes.** All use the always-available crafting grid except the two
14	**furnace** smelting recipes, which need an open furnace.
15	
16	### Building & materials
17	
18	| Result    | Ingredients       |
19	| --------- | ----------------- |
20	| 4 Planks  | 2 Wood            |
21	| 2 Glass   | 4 Sand            |
22	| 2 Brick   | 2 Dirt + 2 Stone  |
23	| 1 Furnace | 8 Cobble          |
24	| 1 Chest   | 8 Planks          |
25	| 1 Bed     | 3 Wool + 3 Planks |
26	| 1 Wool    | 4 String          |
27	
28	### Tools
29	
30	| Result           | Ingredients             |
31	| ---------------- | ----------------------- |
32	| Wood Hoe         | 2 Planks + 1 Wood       |
33	| Wood Pickaxe     | 2 Planks + 2 Wood       |
34	| Stone Pickaxe    | 2 Cobble + 1 Wood       |
35	| Sliver Pickaxe   | 2 Sliver Ore + 1 Wood   |
36	| Ruby Pickaxe     | 2 Ruby Ore + 1 Wood     |
37	| Sapphire Pickaxe | 2 Sapphire Ore + 1 Wood |
38	| Gold Pickaxe     | 2 Gold Ore + 1 Wood     |
39	| Diamond Pickaxe  | 2 Diamond Ore + 1 Wood  |
40	
41	### Weapons
42	
43	| Result         | Ingredients             |
44	| -------------- | ----------------------- |
45	| Knife          | 1 Stone + 1 Wood        |
46	| Wood Sword     | 2 Planks + 1 Wood       |
47	| Stone Sword    | 2 Cobble + 1 Wood       |
48	| Sliver Sword   | 2 Sliver Ore + 1 Wood   |
49	| Ruby Sword     | 2 Ruby Ore + 1 Wood     |
50	| Sapphire Sword | 2 Sapphire Ore + 1 Wood |
51	| Gold Sword     | 2 Gold Ore + 1 Wood     |
52	| Diamond Sword  | 2 Diamond Ore + 1 Wood  |
53	
54	### Armor
55	
56	| Result          | Ingredients                 |
57	| --------------- | --------------------------- |
58	| Helmet          | 4 Sapphire Ore + 1 Ruby Ore |
59	| Face Mask       | 2 Ruby Ore + 2 Sapphire Ore |
60	| Neck Protection | 2 Gold Ore + 1 Sapphire Ore |
61	| Chestplate      | 5 Gold Ore + 2 Sapphire Ore |
62	| Leggings        | 4 Gold Ore + 2 Ruby Ore     |
63	| Boots           | 2 Sapphire Ore + 2 Gold Ore |
64	
65	### Food & smelting
66	
67	| Result         | Ingredients            | Station |
68	| -------------- | ---------------------- | ------- |
69	| Bread          | 3 Wheat                | —       |
70	| Cooked Chicken | Raw Chicken + 1 Planks | Furnace |
71	| Cooked Mutton  | Raw Mutton + 1 Planks  | Furnace |
72	
73	## Blocks
74	
75	**27 block types** (plus air). Hardness is relative break time — higher is slower.
76	"Mine with" is the minimum tool needed; blocks with no requirement break with bare
77	hands or any tool. Bedrock and water cannot be broken.
78	
79	| Block             | Hardness | Mine with      | Notes                                                                                                         |
80	| ----------------- | -------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
81	| Grass             | 2        | any            | Drops dirt; ~20% chance to also drop a seed                                                                   |
82	| Dirt              | 2        | any            | —                                                                                                             |
83	| Sand              | 2        | any            | Common on beaches and in deserts                                                                              |
84	| Snow              | 2        | any            | Mountain peaks                                                                                                |
85	| Leaves            | 2        | any            | From trees; drops dirt                                                                                        |
86	| Cactus            | 2        | any            | Desert decoration                                                                                             |
87	| Glass             | 2        | any            | Crafted from sand                                                                                             |
88	| Wood              | 3        | any            | Tree trunks                                                                                                   |
89	| Planks            | 3        | any            | Crafted from wood                                                                                             |
90	| Stone             | 5        | Wood Pickaxe   | Drops the stone item                                                                                          |
91	| Cobblestone       | 5        | Wood Pickaxe   | Drops cobble (crafting staple)                                                                                |
92	| Brick             | 5        | Wood Pickaxe   | Crafted; also found in houses                                                                                 |
93	| Furnace           | 5        | Wood Pickaxe   | **Interactive** — opens smelting recipes                                                                      |
94	| Chest             | 3        | any            | **Interactive** — 27-slot storage; breaking it spills the contents into your inventory (refused if it's full) |
95	| Sliver Ore        | 7        | Stone Pickaxe  | —                                                                                                             |
96	| Ruby Ore          | 9        | Sliver Pickaxe | —                                                                                                             |
97	| Gold Ore          | 11       | Sliver Pickaxe | —                                                                                                             |
98	| Sapphire Ore      | 12       | Ruby Pickaxe   | —                                                                                                             |
99	| Diamond Ore       | 14       | Ruby Pickaxe   | Deepest, rarest ore                                                                                           |
100	| Bed               | 2        | any            | **Interactive** — sleep & set spawn                                                                           |
101	| Farmland          | 1        | any            | Tilled soil; reverts to dirt when broken                                                                      |
102	| Wheat (stage 0–2) | 1        | any            | Immature crop; drops its seed                                                                                 |
103	| Wheat (stage 3)   | 1        | any            | Mature crop; drops wheat + 1–2 seeds                                                                          |
104	| Bedrock           | —        | unbreakable    | World floor and border                                                                                        |
105	| Water             | —        | —              | Liquid; sea level fills below y≈43                                                                            |
106	
107	## Mobs
108	
109	**6 mob kinds.** Passive animals flee but never attack and can be bred; hostiles
110	hunt at night. Drop counts are inclusive ranges rolled per kill.
111	
112	| Mob      | Type    | HP  | Speed | Detect range | Attack | Cooldown | Drops                      |
113	| -------- | ------- | --- | ----- | ------------ | ------ | -------- | -------------------------- |
114	| Sheep    | passive | 10  | 0.9   | —            | —      | —        | 1–2 Wool, 1 Raw Mutton     |
115	| Chicken  | passive | 7   | 1.2   | —            | —      | —        | 0–2 Feather, 1 Raw Chicken |
116	| Horse    | passive | 14  | 1.4   | —            | —      | —        | 1–2 Leather                |
117	| Zombie   | hostile | 10  | 1.05  | 11           | 3      | 1.35 s   | 1–2 Rotten Flesh           |
118	| Skeleton | hostile | 9   | 1.08  | 12           | 3      | 1.4 s    | 1–2 Bone                   |
119	| Spider   | hostile | 8   | 1.2   | 10           | 2      | 1.1 s    | 0–2 String                 |
120	
121	Spiders are hostile only in the dark (daylight below 0.42); in twilight and day
122	they behave like passives. **Breeding:** feed wheat to a sheep or horse, or seeds
123	to a chicken; babies grow up in ~90 s. Passive population is capped at 24, hostiles
124	at 16.
125	
126	## Items
127	
128	### Tools
129	
130	`minePower` scales break speed; `tier` gates which ore a pickaxe can harvest
131	(stone/cobble/brick ≥ 1, sliver ≥ 2, ruby & gold ≥ 3, sapphire & diamond ≥ 4).
132	
133	| Item             | Mine power | Tier | Durability |
134	| ---------------- | ---------- | ---- | ---------- |
135	| Wood Hoe         | 1.0        | 0    | 90         |
136	| Wood Pickaxe     | 1.05       | 1    | 70         |
137	| Stone Pickaxe    | 1.55       | 2    | 140        |
138	| Sliver Pickaxe   | 2.2        | 3    | 240        |
139	| Ruby Pickaxe     | 2.8        | 4    | 340        |
140	| Sapphire Pickaxe | 3.3        | 5    | 430        |
141	| Gold Pickaxe     | 3.8        | 6    | 520        |
142	| Diamond Pickaxe  | 4.4        | 7    | 700        |
143	
144	### Weapons
145	
146	A bare fist deals 6 damage for comparison.
147	
148	| Item           | Attack | Durability |
149	| -------------- | ------ | ---------- |
150	| Knife          | 9      | 50         |
151	| Wood Sword     | 13     | 80         |
152	| Stone Sword    | 18     | 160        |
153	| Sliver Sword   | 24     | 260        |
154	| Ruby Sword     | 31     | 360        |
155	| Sapphire Sword | 35     | 450        |
156	| Gold Sword     | 40     | 540        |
157	| Diamond Sword  | 47     | 720        |
158	
159	### Armor
160	
161	Each piece occupies its own equip slot. Defense reduces incoming damage (a hit
162	always deals at least 1).
163	
164	| Item            | Slot            | Defense | Durability |
165	| --------------- | --------------- | ------- | ---------- |
166	| Helmet          | helmet          | 2       | 260        |
167	| Face Mask       | face_mask       | 2       | 220        |
168	| Neck Protection | neck_protection | 2       | 230        |
169	| Chestplate      | chestplate      | 4       | 420        |
170	| Leggings        | leggings        | 3       | 340        |
171	| Boots           | boots           | 2       | 250        |
172	
173	### Food
174	
175	`Eat` (`F`) restores the listed hunger.
176	
177	| Item           | Hunger restored | Source             |
178	| -------------- | --------------- | ------------------ |
179	| Cooked Chicken | 8               | Smelt raw chicken  |
180	| Cooked Mutton  | 8               | Smelt raw mutton   |
181	| Food           | 7               | Generic food item  |
182	| Bread          | 6               | Craft from 3 wheat |
183	| Raw Chicken    | 3               | Chicken drop       |
184	| Raw Mutton     | 3               | Sheep drop         |
185	| Rotten Flesh   | 2               | Zombie drop        |
186	
187	### Materials
188	
189	Crafting ingredients with no direct use on their own: **Wool**, **Feather**,
190	**Bone**, **Leather**, **String** (mob drops), and **Wheat Seeds** / **Wheat**
191	(from farming and grass).
192

1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	## [0.6.0] - 2026-06-13
8	
9	### Added
10	
11	- **Chests & persistent storage**: craft a chest (8 planks), place it, and right-click it to open a **27-slot** storage grid above your inventory — the long-standing "everything rides in 36 slots on your person" gap is closed
12	  - Introduces the game's first **block-entity** layer: a chest's contents live in `state.containers` (a `Map` keyed by the block's voxel index, the same space as the block diff). The right-click opens it via the existing interact precedence (after bed/furnace), and the same click-one-slot-then-another interaction now spans both grids through a new `moveStack` command (chest slots offset by `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE`); pure cross-array `moveStack`/`tryInsertSlots` helpers join `lib/game/inventory.ts`
13	  - **Breaking a chest spills its contents** back into your inventory (tool/armor durability preserved) and returns the chest item; if there isn't room for everything the break is refused (a `breakBlocked` toast) so nothing is lost — consistent with the game's no-ground-item loot model
14	  - **Save format v4** (additive — `SAVE_KEY` unchanged, v1/v2/v3 saves still load): a new optional `blockEntities` field persists non-empty chests; `migrateSaveV3toV4` is a pure version bump and `readSave` chains v1→v2→v3→v4. **No worldgen impact** (chests are never generated). New `chest` block/item, an 8-planks recipe, a `CHEST_SLOTS` tunable, a generated chest atlas tile, and a synthesized open-creak sound
15	- **Juice & atmosphere pass**: the world now reacts and breathes, all via procedural rendering/audio (zero new assets) with **no save-format or worldgen impact**
16	  - **Particles**: a pure, unit-tested structure-of-arrays particle pool (`lib/game/render/particlePool.ts`) wrapped by a single `THREE.Points` draw call (`particleSystem.ts`) whose attributes refill each frame; a small shader draws each particle as a soft round sprite that fades over its life (no texture). The renderer gained `handleEvent(event, state)`, wired into the event drain beside `audio.handleEvent`: breaking a block throws shards tinted from `BLOCK_COLORS`, placing puffs, mob death bursts in the mob's body color, eating drops crumbs, and jumping/landing kick dust (landing scales with impact). Footstep dust spawns from a stride-distance accumulator. The three positioned events (`blockBroken`/`blockPlaced`/`mobDied`) gained additive `x,y,z` fields so bursts land correctly
17	  - **Night sky**: a deterministic 800-point star field that fades in at dusk, sprite discs for the moon and a now-visible sun riding opposite ends of the sun arc, and a drifting cloud sheet from a seamlessly tiling canvas-noise mask (`starField.ts`, `skyView.ts`). All camera-following and fog-exempt; opacities ramp off `daylight`
18	  - **Weather**: a pure, deterministic system (`engine/systems/weather.ts`) sets a **transient** `state.weather` (never serialized) — time splits into windows, a seeded hash decides precipitation, and biome picks snow (mountains) / clear (desert, ocean) / rain. It drives a camera-following precipitation field (`precipitation.ts`), an overcast sky tint + dimmed light + nearer fog in `syncDayNight`, and a synthesized looping rain bed on the audio graph (`rainLoop.ts`). Strictly cosmetic — spawn/daylight balance is untouched. New `WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS` / `WEATHER_RAIN_FRACTION` tunables
19	- **UI polish pass for a more authentic Minecraft look**: the interface now reads closer to real Minecraft across five fronts
20	  - **Pixel font**: UI text switches from the generic monospace stack to **Monocraft**, an open-source Minecraft-style face (SIL OFL 1.1) self-hosted via `next/font/local` from a committed woff2 in `app/fonts/` and exposed as the `--mc-font` CSS variable. Its coding ligatures are disabled so UI text stays literal. The monospace stack stays as the fallback and is pinned on the F3 debug readout, which relies on column alignment. This bundled font file is a deliberate, documented exception to the zero-binary-asset rule (the only one)
21	  - **Textured GUI chrome**: panels, inventory slots, recipe entries, and buttons gain a faint generated grain instead of flat fills. A new `grayGrainTileUrl` generator in `lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts` installs `--mc-tile-panel/well/button` as CSS variables (sunken bias on the slot well); the CSS layers each tile over the existing flat color, so bevels are untouched and SSR/pre-install falls back to the solid color
22	  - **Item tooltips**: hovering hotbar, inventory, and recipe entries now shows a cursor-following, near-black tooltip with a violet gradient border (new `useItemTooltip` hook in `components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx`) instead of the browser's native `title=`. Durable items show a gray "Durability x / y" line and locked recipes show "Requires <station>". The tooltip is `pointer-events:none` and `aria-hidden`, so it never blocks the pointer-lock click and accessible names (the `aria-label`s) are unchanged
23	  - **Depth**: a shared `.menu-backdrop` dims the frozen world behind the inventory and pause menu (the dimming moved off `.pause-overlay` so it no longer double-darkens), plus an always-on screen-edge `.vignette` over the 3D view (tunable via `--mc-vignette`)
24	  - **Motion**: a centered pop-in for the inventory panel, a fade-in for the pause overlay, quick hover/press feedback on slots and buttons, and a small rise on the hotbar item-name pop — all disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`
25	  - No save-format or worldgen impact
26	- **Pickup & status toasts**: because mob loot drops straight into inventory storage (no ground item), kills could feel like they paid out nothing. A kill now shows a brief on-screen toast just above the hotbar (e.g. "+2 Wool, +1 Raw Mutton"). The same in-game toast also surfaces the sleep-denied messages ("You can only sleep at night" / "Monsters are nearby"), which previously only rendered inside the pause menu and so were invisible during play
27	- **Animal breeding**: right-click a sheep or horse with wheat (or a chicken with seeds) to put it "in love"; two in-love adults of the same kind standing close together spawn a baby that follows the parents, can't be farmed for drops, and grows to full size after ~90 seconds. This makes mob drops renewable, closing the loop back to combat loot. The passive population is capped (24) and feeding costs crops, so it stays bounded
28	  - New `MobState.fedTimer`/`ageTimer`, a `breeding` system ticked after mob AI, and a shared `findAimedMobIndex` so feeding and attacking use the same "what's in my crosshair" rule. Feeding joins the right-click precedence ahead of block interaction. New fed/bred sounds; babies render at 55% scale. Breeding state is session-only (mobs are never saved). No save-format or worldgen impact
29	- **Furnace & cooking**: craft a furnace (8 cobble) and right-click it to open the crafting panel in furnace mode, which unlocks smelting recipes — raw chicken or mutton + 1 planks (the fuel) cook into the cooked version, restoring 8 hunger versus 3 raw. Smelting recipes show as locked ("Requires Furnace") until a furnace is open
30	  - Reuses the Phase 2 interact system and the existing crafting panel — no separate furnace UI. Recipes gained an optional `station` field; the gate is enforced engine-side in the `craft` command (UI gating alone is spoofable). New `Furnace` block with a glowing-mouth atlas tile, `cooked_chicken`/`cooked_mutton` items, and a smelt sound. No save-format or worldgen impact
31	- **Farming & food**: craft a wood hoe (2 planks + 1 wood), right-click grass or dirt to till it into farmland, then right-click farmland with seeds to plant wheat. Crops grow through four stages over ~2.5 minutes and, when mature, harvest into wheat plus 1–2 seeds; an immature crop just returns its seed. Craft 3 wheat into bread (restores 6 hunger)
32	  - Seeds come from breaking grass (20% chance per block) — `addBlockDrop` now rolls a per-block `rollBlockDrops` table (`lib/game/items.ts`) instead of a single fixed drop
33	  - New **random-tick system** (`lib/game/engine/systems/randomTicks.ts`): each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers — the extensible basis for crop growth (and future saplings / grass spread). Crops are solid full-cube blocks (so they can be targeted and harvested) with each growth stage its own `BlockId`, which means they persist through the existing block-diff save with **no save-format change**
34	  - New `Farmland` + `WheatStage0..3` blocks, `wood_hoe`/`seeds`/`wheat`/`bread` items with generated sprites, and till/plant sounds. The right-click "use held item" step joins the interact precedence (after block interaction, before placement). No worldgen changes
35	- **Beds & sleeping**: craft a bed (3 wool + 3 planks), place it, and right-click it at night to skip to morning — the screen fades to black, the day clock jumps to a fresh dawn, and the bed becomes your respawn point (respawn falls back to a random land point if the bed is gone). Sleeping is refused during the day or with a hostile within 12 blocks, with an on-screen reason
36	  - New **right-click interact system** (`lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts`): a fixed precedence in the `placeBlock` command runs block interaction before placement, so interactive blocks (beds now; furnaces later) take the click instead of getting a block placed on them. New `Bed` block + `bed` item, synthesized sleep/wake sounds, and a `SleepOverlay` fade component
37	  - **Save format v3** (additive — `SAVE_KEY` unchanged, v1/v2 saves still load): time of day (`dayClock`), `hearts`, `hunger`, and the bed `spawnPoint` now persist. Previously all four reset on reload, so sleeping through the night wouldn't have survived a reload. `readSave` chains the migrations v1 → v2 → v3
38	- **Mob loot & drops**: mobs now drop kind-specific items when they die instead of the old flat "cobble for hostiles, food for everything else" rule. Sheep drop wool + raw mutton, chickens feather + raw chicken, horses leather, zombies rotten flesh, skeletons bone, spiders string — counts are randomized per the engine's RNG (`lib/game/mobLoot.ts`). A new `mobDied` engine event plays a synthesized death thud
39	  - New items: `wool`, `feather`, `bone`, `leather`, `string` (crafting materials) and `rotten_flesh`, `raw_chicken`, `raw_mutton` (edible food), each with a generated 16×16 sprite (zero-asset)
40	  - **Per-food hunger**: food now restores a value carried on the item itself rather than one global constant. The generic `food` item still restores 7; raw meats restore 3, rotten flesh 2. New `food` and `material` item kinds; the legacy `food` item is reinterpreted (old saves load unchanged — saves store only item id + count)
41	  - New recipe: 4 string → 1 wool, so spider drops feed the wool supply (and, later, beds)
42	  - No save-format or worldgen impact
43	  - Selection recolors the body live (material color swap, no rebuild) and persists as a player preference under its own localStorage key (`minecraft_skin_v1`), separate from the world save — it survives world resets. No save-format or worldgen impact
44	- **Camera view toggle (V)**: cycles first-person → third-person rear → third-person front, like Minecraft's F5 (V instead, because F5 reloads the page in browsers)
45	  - New humanoid player body (head, torso, two arms, two legs) in the zero-asset box-mesh style, visible only in third person — walk gait scaled by speed, a chop animation on attacks and while mining, the look pitch on the head, and the held hotbar item rendered in the right hand via the shared item-model builder
46	  - The third-person camera boom raycasts against terrain and clamps so walls never occlude the player; the front view flips the heading and inverts the tilt while mouse control of the player stays unchanged
47	  - Gameplay is intentionally eye-relative in every mode (mining/placing reach, combat aim, audio panning are unaffected); the crosshair stays centered, matching Minecraft
48	  - The view mode is session-only (resets to first-person on reload). No save-format or worldgen impact
49	
50	### Documentation
51	
52	- **Added `docs/manual.md`**, a player-facing guide: getting started / your first day, the full control map, survival (health, hunger gates, combat, armor, death), the day-night thresholds, mining with tool-tier gating, crafting and smelting, the mob roster and breeding, farming, beds and sleeping, the procedural audio, and saving. Linked from the README and AGENTS.md
53	- **Added `docs/reference.md`**, scannable tables cross-checked against the code: all 31 recipes, the 26 block types (hardness + tool gate), the 6 mobs (stats + drop ranges), and item stats for tools, weapons, armor, and food
54	- **Added `docs/tuning.md`**, a contributor balance guide that groups the `config.ts` tunables by gameplay effect (player feel, survival pressure, danger, progression, farming/breeding, persistence/rendering), names the system that reads each, and flags the save-sensitive ones (`SAVE_KEY`, inventory layout)

Perfect! I now have all the information needed. Let me provide a comprehensive report:

## Exploration Complete: Monecraft World Generation Architecture

### 1. **World Generation Module — `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts`**

**Generation Flow** (lines 63-82, deterministically ordered):
```
generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld):
  1. generateTerrain(world)          # Fill base terrain, biomes, heights
  2. carveCaves(world, rand)         # Carve 230 main caves + 150 chambers
  3. placeWater(world)               # Fill sea level (y=43)
  4. placeBeaches(world)             # Sand shorelines near water
  5. placeOres(world, rand)          # 5 ore types in veins
  6. placeTrees(world, rand)         # 9000 tree placement attempts
  7. placeCacti(world)               # Desert cacti (hash-gated, not rand)
  8. placeStructures(world, rand)    # Houses + future dungeons
```

**Key Constants** (GEN object, lines 18-46):
- `seaLevel: 43` — water fill height
- `caveCount: 230` — wandering cave tunnels
- `caveChamberCount: 150` — spherical chamber additions
- `houseCount: 120` — current structure count
- `treeAttempts: 9000`
- Biomes config with baseHeight, noiseScale, treeChance per BiomeId

**The placeStructures Hook** (lines 320-326):
```typescript
function placeStructures(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number): void {
  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.houseCount; i += 1) {
    const cx = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 24));
    const cz = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24));
    placeHouse(world, cx, cz);
  }
}
```

**Current House Placement** (lines 328-369):
- 120 houses placed via random X,Z coordinate sampling (12-500 range, leaving 12-block border)
- Validates flat terrain: checks ±1 block height variation in 7x7 area around center
- Places 7x7 cobblestone floor, 4-block-tall brick walls with windows, wood corner posts, plank roof
- Early-returns on unsuitable terrain, so actual placement count may be lower than 120
- Uses `world.highestSolidY()` to find floor level
- Pattern: validation loop, then 3 nested coordinate-based placement loops filling layers

**Extension Pattern for Dungeons**:
To add dungeon room placement, you'd follow the same pattern:
1. Add `dungeonCount: N` to GEN constants
2. Create `placeDungeons(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number)` function
3. Call it from `generateWorld()` (likely after `placeStructures` or between caves and structures)
4. Random XZ sampling with validation of suitable placement (you could carve rooms into caves or use terrain-height validation)
5. Use nested loops with `world.set()` to fill block IDs

---

### 2. **Voxel World Representation — `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/voxelWorld.ts`**

**Core Storage** (lines 11-24):
```typescript
class VoxelWorld {
  readonly sizeX: number;  // 512
  readonly sizeY: number;  // 150
  readonly sizeZ: number;  // 512
  readonly seed: number;
  readonly blocks: Uint8Array;  // Linear 1D array, length = sizeX * sizeY * sizeZ
}
```

**Voxel Index System** (line 27, critical for save-format):
```typescript
index(x: number, y: number, z: number): number {
  return x + z * this.sizeX + y * this.sizeX * this.sizeZ;
}
```
- **Coordinate order**: X-major innermost, then Z, then Y
- **Total voxels**: 512 × 150 × 512 = 39,321,600 bytes (Uint8Array)
- Full world at seed 1337 has SHA-256 hash: `788cb3a2952929975d18d11a47e789d324f7df76be47912a0b4221b07ad88b58`

**Get/Set Operations** (lines 34-42):
```typescript
get(x, y, z): number         // Returns BlockId, auto-clamps out-of-bounds to Air
set(x, y, z, block: BlockId) // Writes to index, respects bounds
inBounds(x, y, z): boolean   // Boundary check before indexing
highestSolidY(x, z): number  // Scan Y downward to find top solid block
isSolid(x, y, z): boolean    // Air and Water are not solid
```

**Biome Field** (lines 56-72):
```typescript
getBiome(x: number, z: number): BiomeId
```
- Deterministic sine-based multi-octave field per seed
- Returns one of: Plains, Desert, Ocean, Forest, Mountains
- Used in terrain generation and structure validation
- **Important**: changes to this function break every existing save (documented in comments)

### 3. **Caves — Already Fully Implemented** (`carveCaves()`, lines 139-188)

**Cave Structure**:
- **230 main cave tunnels**: randomly seeded starting points (12-500 range), wander via yaw/pitch changes
- **150 chamber additions**: secondary large spheres (radius 5.4-12.8) added to existing caves
- **Carving method**: `carveSphere(cx, cy, cz, radius)` clears non-bedrock blocks to Air within radius
- **Path algorithm**: Brownian-motion-like walk through 3D space, direction drifts per step
- **Boundary protection**: caves break/stop at borders (x/z < 8, x/z > 504, y < 2, y > 146)

**How Underground Rooms Could Integrate**:
- Dungeons could carve larger rooms alongside caves using the same `carveSphere()` or a rectangular carving function
- Place dungeon rooms _before_ caves (re-order in `generateWorld()`), so caves naturally intersect them
- Or place after caves and skip carving through already-air blocks (check `world.get()` before carving)
- Match cave carving bounds: leave 1-block buffer from bedrock (y=0) and walls

**Re-baselining Policy** (from `docs/testing.md` lines 47-60):

- **Only rebaseline in 2 cases**:
  1. **Deliberate worldgen change** — flag in PR/CHANGELOG, bump `SAVE_KEY` to discard old saves
  2. **Bun version bump** — `Math.sin()` results are engine-defined; pin Bun version in CI together with new digests
- **What breaks saves**: changes to `index()` formula, `getBiome()`, generation order, RNG, or GEN constants

### 5. **Seeded RNG & Hash Utilities**

**LCG RNG** (lines 64-72, embedded in `generateWorld()`):
```typescript
const rand = (() => {
  let t = (world.seed >>> 0) + 0x6d2b79f5;
  return () => {
    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
  };
})();
```
- **Type**: Seeded 32-bit integer-multiply-rotate LCG
- **Reproducibility**: Deterministic per seed; same seed → same sequence forever
- **Range**: 0 to 1 (exclusive), suitable for `rand() * range` patterns
- **Passed to**: `carveCaves()`, `placeOres()`, `placeTrees()`, `placeStructures()`

**Hash Functions** (not seeded, for spatial determinism):
```typescript
function hash2D(x: number, z: number): number {
  // Sine-based hash, returns [0, 1)
  const n = Math.sin(x * 127.1 + z * 311.7) * 43758.5453123;
  return n - Math.floor(n);
}

function smoothNoise2D(x: number, z: number, seed: number): number {
  // Multi-octave sine-cosine field for terrain height variation
  const s = seed * 0.013;
  return (4 sine-cosine terms with different scales and phases);
}
```
- Used for **deterministic spatial features** (cacti placement via `hash2D`, terrain height via `smoothNoise2D`)
- Not consumed from the RNG stream, so placement density is stable even if RNG count changes
- **Biome field** (in `voxelWorld.ts`) also uses similar multi-octave sines per seed

### **Key Takeaway for Dungeon Planning**

The extension point is clear: **`placeStructures()` is where you'd add dungeon placement**. The pattern is:
1. Loop `GEN.dungeonCount` times
2. Random XZ within safe bounds (12-500 for 512-wide map)
3. Validate placement (terrain flatness, height range, cave proximity if desired)
4. Use nested loops with `world.set()` to place blocks
5. Add `dungeonCount` to GEN constants
6. Call `placeDungeons()` from `generateWorld()` in the desired generation order
7. On any meaningful change, the SHA-256 hash will fail — expected, rebaseline per policy
8. Consider bumping SAVE_KEY if this is a user-facing feature (to preserve existing saves)

## Comprehensive Mob & Spawning System Exploration Report

### 1. Mob Spawning System

**Primary files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/spawnDirector.ts` (spawn behavior)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/spawn.ts` (spawn position selection)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts` (tunables)

**How mobs are spawned:**

The spawning has two phases:
1. **Initial world population** (`spawnInitialMobs`, lines 52-68 in spawnDirector.ts): On world creation, calls `spawnMobGroup()` for six groups (sheep/chicken/horse × passive, zombie/skeleton/spider × hostile). Passives spawn in a ring `RENDER_RADIUS * 1.2` around the player; hostiles in a tighter ring `RENDER_RADIUS * 0.7`.

2. **Night spawning director** (`tickHostileSpawnDirector`, lines 71-94 in spawnDirector.ts): Every `HOSTILE_SPAWN_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (10 seconds, from config.ts line 64), if daylight is below `HOSTILE_SPAWN_BELOW_DAYLIGHT` (0.28), spawns 1-2 random hostile mobs (zombie/skeleton/spider) around the player at `RENDER_RADIUS * 0.85` radius, up to the cap of `HOSTILE_CAP` (16).

**How spawn positions are chosen:**

Two key functions in spawn.ts:

1. **`randomLandPointNear()`** (lines 71-88): Searches for a safe spawn up to 50 attempts within a radius of a center point (centerX, centerZ). On each attempt, it:
   - Picks a random point within the radius (±radius box, then clamps to world bounds)
   - Checks if the surface is dry (not flooded water)
   - Returns if valid, else falls back to `randomLandPoint()` (global search)

2. **`findSpawnOnLand()`** (lines 20-54): Deterministic spiral search used on world load. Validates:
   - Solid floor below (y-1)
   - Two air blocks for body/head (y, y+1)
   - No water
   - Neighbors within ±2 blocks height (gentle slope)
   - Prefers Plains biome (falls back to any biome)
   - Searches up to maxRadius = 300 blocks in spiral

**For a localized spawner block:**

You can adapt `spawnMobGroup()` and `randomLandPointNear()` directly. Create a spawner system that:
- Tracks spawner positions in state (new field in `GameState`)
- On a per-tick interval, checks if the player is within a trigger radius of a spawner
- Calls `spawnMobGroup(state, { kind: "zombie", hostile: true, count: 1-2, centerX: spawner.x, centerZ: spawner.z, radius: 8 }, rng, surfaceYAt)` to spawn locally near the spawner block
- Cap spawns per spawner and globally per spawn-source to balance with the global `HOSTILE_CAP`

const mob: MobState = {
  id: state.nextMobId,
  kind: args.kind,
  hostile: args.hostile,
  hp: template.hp,

};

**Persistence:**

Mobs are **session-only** — never persisted in saves. `GameState.mobs: MobState[]` is not serialized. On load, `spawnInitialMobs()` is called to repopulate the world.

### 3. Engine Step Order & Systems

**Per-frame step order** (GameEngine.ts, lines 162-206):

1. Pause gate (if paused, return early)
2. Stuck detection / auto-unstuck
3. Death check + respawn countdown (if dead, only mobs tick)
4. Sleep gate (if sleeping, just tick the sleep timer)
5. `tickPlayerMotion()` — movement & physics
6. `tickHungerDrain()` — hunger from movement
7. `tickHealthRegen()` — health recovery
8. `tickMining()` — mining progress & block breaking
9. `tickDayNight()` — day clock
10. `tickWeather()` — cosmetic weather state
11. `tickRandomBlocks()` — crop growth
12. **`tickHostileSpawnDirector()`** — spawn new hostiles at night
13. **`tickMobs()`** — mob AI: wander, aggro, flee, attack, burn
14. `tickBreeding()` — animal love timers & baby maturity

Combat and interactions run on **command** (not per-frame):
- `tryFeedAimedMob()`, `tryInteractBlock()`, `tryUseHeldItem()` in the `placeBlock` command precedence
- `tryAttackMob()` in the `attack` command

**Where a "spawner" system would slot in:**

Insert between `tickRandomBlocks()` and `tickHostileSpawnDirector()` — or even merge into `tickHostileSpawnDirector()` as an additional spawn source. The spawner system would read `state.world` (to find placed spawner blocks) and spawn mobs via `spawnMobGroup()`.

**State & config access pattern:**

Each system function signature is:
```typescript
export function tickFoo(state: GameState, dt: number, ...deps): void {
  // Read/write state directly; use deps for RNG, surface lookup, event emission
}
```

For example, `tickHostileSpawnDirector(state, dt, rng, surfaceYAt)` reads `state.daylight`, `state.player.position`, `state.mobs`, and writes `state.timers.hostileSpawnTimer`, then calls `spawnMobGroup()` to mutate `state.mobs`.

**Pattern for adding a new tunable:**

1. Define the constant at the top of config.ts: `export const MY_SPAWNER_SPAWN_RATE = 5;`
2. Import it in the system that uses it: `import { MY_SPAWNER_SPAWN_RATE } from "@/lib/game/config";`
3. Use it in the system: `if (state.timers.mySpawnerTimer > MY_SPAWNER_SPAWN_RATE) { ... }`

No registration or lookup — just direct imports. The engine initializes timers in `createTimers()` (state.ts, lines 124-138), so add a new timer field to `GameTimers` type and initialize it in `createTimers()`.

### 5. Interact System & Events

**Primary files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts` (block interactions)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/state.ts` (GameEvent definition)

**Interactive block pattern** (interact.ts, lines 22-54):

Interactive blocks are tagged in `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` map:
```typescript
export type InteractiveKind = "bed" | "furnace" | "chest";

export const INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS: Partial<Record<BlockId, InteractiveKind>> = {
  [BlockId.Bed]: "bed",
  [BlockId.Furnace]: "furnace",
  [BlockId.Chest]: "chest"
};

export function tryInteractBlock(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent): boolean {
  // Raycast from player eye to aimed block
  // If the block is interactive, call its handler
  if (kind === "bed") return interactBed(state, emit, result.hit.x, result.hit.y, result.hit.z);
  if (kind === "furnace") return interactFurnace(state, emit);
  if (kind === "chest") return interactChest(state, emit, result.hit.x, result.hit.y, result.hit.z);
  return false;
}
```

**To add a spawner block interaction:**

1. Add `BlockId.Spawner` to `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` with kind `"spawner"`
2. Implement `interactSpawner(state, emit)` that emits a UI event (e.g., open a spawner config panel)
3. The engine event system will drain it; the audio/rendering layer can respond

**GameEvent system** (state.ts, lines 191-215):

Events are one-shot payloads emitted via `emit()` (a callback passed to systems). The engine collects them and the shell drains them each frame:

```typescript
export type GameEvent =
  | { type: "died" }
  | { type: "respawned" }
  | { type: "blockBroken"; blockId: BlockId; x: number; y: number; z: number }
  | { type: "blockPlaced"; blockId: BlockId; x: number; y: number; z: number }
  | { type: "mobAttacked"; kind: MobKind }
  | { type: "mobHit"; kind: MobKind }
  | { type: "mobDied"; kind: MobKind; x: number; y: number; z: number }
  | { type: "mobFed"; kind: MobKind }
  | { type: "mobBred"; kind: MobKind }
  // ... more events
```

**For spawner events:**

Add to the union:
```typescript
| { type: "spawnerActivated"; x: number; y: number; z: number; kind: MobKind }
| { type: "mobSpawned"; kind: MobKind; x: number; y: number; z: number }
```

Then emit them in your spawner system:
```typescript
emit({ type: "spawnerActivated", x: spawner.x, y: spawner.y, z: spawner.z, kind: "zombie" });
// Later in the audio/renderer drain:
for (const event of engine.consumeEvents()) {
  if (event.type === "spawnerActivated") {
    // Emit particle burst or sound at (event.x, event.y, event.z)
  }
}
```

**Event emission pattern in systems** (example from breeding.ts, line 45):
```typescript
emit({ type: "mobBred", kind: a.kind });
```

### Concrete Recommendations for Dungeon/Spawner Feature

1. **Spawner block entity**: Add to `state.containers` or a new `state.spawners: Map<voxelIndex, SpawnerState>` tracking active spawners, their kind, cooldowns, and lifetime spawns.

2. **SpawnerState type**: Consider fields like `{ kind: MobKind, activationRadius: number, spawnCooldown: number, spawnTimer: number, maxSpawns: number }`.

3. **New system**: Create `systems/spawnerDirector.ts` alongside `spawnDirector.ts`. Insert its tick between random blocks and the global hostile director (step 8–9).

4. **Right-click interaction**: Add `BlockId.Spawner` to `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` with handler `interactSpawner()` that emits a `{ type: "spawnerUI" }` event for a future UI panel.

5. **Spawn position search**: Reuse `randomLandPointNear()` directly with the spawner position as center and a small radius (e.g., 8 blocks).

6. **Event emission**: Define `{ type: "spawnerActivated", x, y, z, kind }` to trigger particle/sound in the renderer/audio.

7. **Population cap interaction**: Either increase `HOSTILE_CAP` when dungeons are active, or make spawner-spawned mobs count separately (e.g., track `state.timers.dungeonHostileCount`).

## Thorough Exploration Report: Loot & Container Systems

I've completed a systematic exploration of the codebase. Here are the key findings organized by your focus areas:

### 1. Block-Entity / Container Layer

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/state.ts` (lines 96-99)

The container state is defined as:
```typescript
/** Chest contents (block-entities) keyed by the block's voxel index. */
containers: Map<number, InventorySlot[]>;
/** Voxel index of the chest open in the inventory panel, or null. */
openContainerIndex: number | null;
```

**Key Constants:**
- `CHEST_SLOTS = 27` (9x3 grid, like Minecraft single chest) — `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts:32`
- `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE = 1000` (slot index offset for container moves) — `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/commands.ts:11`
- `MAX_STACK_SIZE = 99` — `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts:30`

**Chest Opening & Lazy Initialization:** 
In `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts` (lines 56-69), when a chest is clicked, it creates an empty slot array lazily:
```typescript
function interactChest(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent, x: number, y: number, z: number): boolean {
  const idx = state.world.index(x, y, z);
  if (!state.containers.has(idx)) {
    state.containers.set(
      idx,
      Array.from({ length: CHEST_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot())
    );
  }
  state.openContainerIndex = idx;
  state.inventoryOpen = true;
  emit({ type: "openedContainer" });
  return true;
}
```

**Pre-Filling Chests at Runtime:** To pre-fill a chest with items at worldgen time or first-load, you would:
1. During worldgen in `placeStructures()` (or a new `plungeon placeDungeon()` function), after placing chest blocks
2. Call `state.world.index(x, y, z)` to get the voxel index
3. Create a slot array and populate it using `createSlot()` / `createEmptySlot()` helpers from `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts`
4. Insert into `state.containers.set(index, slots)` **before** the player ever interacts with the chest

The key insight: **containers aren't created by worldgen—they're lazily materialized on first interaction**. To pre-populate, you'd need to inject them into the `containers` Map during engine initialization after `generateWorld()` runs.

### 2. Save Format & Persistence

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (lines 92-101)

Save format v4 definition:
```typescript
export type SaveData = Omit<SaveDataV3, "version"> & {
  version: 4;
  blockEntities?: SavedContainer[];
};

export type SavedContainer = { index: number; slots: SavedSlot[] };
export type SavedSlot = { id: string | null; count: number; durability?: number };
```

**Migration Chain:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts`
- `migrateSaveV1toV2()` (lines 16–43): Packs 40→36 slots
- `migrateSaveV2toV3()` (lines 50–52): Pure version bump (adds optional dayClock, hearts, hunger, spawnPoint)
- `migrateSaveV3toV4()` (lines 58–60): Pure version bump (adds optional blockEntities array)

**Serialization & Restoration:**

File: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts` (lines 109–132)

```typescript
/** Snapshots non-empty chest containers for persistence (empty chests carry no data). */
export function serializeContainers(containers: Map<number, InventorySlot[]>): SavedContainer[] {
  const out: SavedContainer[] = [];
  for (const [index, slots] of containers) {
    if (!slots.some((slot) => slot.id && slot.count > 0)) continue;
    out.push({ index, slots: inventorySlotsSnapshot(slots) });
  }
  return out;
}

export function readContainers(save: SaveData): Array<{ index: number; slots: InventorySlot[] }> {
  if (!Array.isArray(save.blockEntities)) return [];
  const out: Array<{ index: number; slots: InventorySlot[] }> = [];
  for (const entry of save.blockEntities) {
    if (!entry || !Number.isFinite(entry.index) || !Array.isArray(entry.slots)) continue;
    const slots = Array.from({ length: CHEST_SLOTS }, (_, i) => restoreSlot(entry.slots[i]));
    out.push({ index: entry.slots });
  }
  return out;
}
```

**Key Invariants for Dungeon Chests:**
- Only non-empty containers are persisted (empty chests save no data)
- `readContainers()` pads slots to `CHEST_SLOTS` length and validates each slot's item ID exists
- **Critical:** In `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` (lines 134–139), restored containers are only loaded if the voxel index still holds a `BlockId.Chest`:
  ```typescript
  for (const { index, slots } of readContainers(save)) {
    if (index >= 0 && index < world.blocks.length && world.blocks[index] === BlockId.Chest) {
      this.state.containers.set(index, slots);
    }
  }
  ```
  This means dungeon-generated chests will persist correctly as long as the chest block itself isn't broken.

**Block-Diff Save Model Interaction:** The save stores block deltas (changes) separately from container contents. Dungeon chests placed during worldgen won't appear in the block-diff index—they're part of the deterministic terrain baseline. When a player breaks a chest block, it's recorded in `changes[]`. On load, the engine:
1. Regenerates the world from seed
2. Applies the `changes` block-diff
3. Loads containers only for indices that still hold `BlockId.Chest`

This ensures dungeon chests persist as long as they're not broken.

### 3. Loot Patterns

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobLoot.ts` (complete file is 53 lines)

**Mob Loot Table Structure:**
```typescript
export type MobDrop = {
  itemId: string;
  min: number;
  max: number;
  /** 0..1 probability the entry drops at all; omitted means always. */
  chance?: number;
};

export const MOB_DROPS: Record<MobKind, MobDrop[]> = {
  sheep: [
    { itemId: "wool", min: 1, max: 2 },
    { itemId: "raw_mutton", min: 1, max: 1 }
  ],
  // ... etc
};
```

**Rolling Loot:**
```typescript
export function rollMobDrops(kind: MobKind, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];
  for (const entry of MOB_DROPS[kind]) {
    if (entry.chance !== undefined && clampUnit(rng()) >= entry.chance) continue;
    const span = entry.max - entry.min + 1;
    const count = entry.min + Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * span);
    if (count > 0) drops.push({ itemId: entry.id, count });
  }
  return drops;
}
```

**Block Drops (similar pattern):** File: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts` (lines 178–196)

```typescript
export function rollBlockDrops(block: BlockId, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];
  const base = BLOCK_TO_SLOT[block];
  if (base) drops.push({ itemId: base, count: 1 });

  if (block === BlockId.Grass && rng() < GRASS_SEED_DROP_CHANCE) {
    drops.push({ itemId: "seeds", count: 1 });
  }
  if (block === BlockId.WheatStage3) {
    drops.push({ itemId: "wheat", count: 1 });
    drops.push({ itemId: "seeds", count: 1 + Math.floor(rng() * 2) });
  }
  return drops;
}
```

**For Dungeon Loot Tables:** You can follow this exact pattern—define a tiered loot table as an array of drop entries with min/max/chance, then `rollDungeonLoot(difficulty, rng)` to produce the items to place in a chest.

**Atlas Tile Generation (Zero-Asset Principle):** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/atlas.ts` (lines 29–97)

Tiles are generated procedurally at runtime per-block:
```typescript
export function tileIndexFor(block: number, face: "top" | "side" | "bottom"): number {
  const faceId = face === "top" ? 0 : face === "side" ? 1 : 2;
  return block * ATLAS_FACE_VARIANTS + faceId;
}
```

Each tile is a 16x16 pixel canvas texture painted with the color from `BLOCK_COLORS[blockId]`, then procedurally toned and noise-textured:
- Top face: `tone(base, 1.08)` (lightened)
- Bottom face: `tone(base, 0.96)` (darkened)
- Side: base color with Perlin-like noise
- Special rules (e.g., ore specks, chest latches, furnace glow) are hardcoded per block

The atlas automatically includes all blocks with entries in `BLOCK_COLORS`. Adding a new block just requires a color entry—no asset files needed.

### 5. Inventory Helpers

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts` (complete file is 287 lines)

**Key Reusable Functions for Filling Loot Chests:**

```typescript
export function adjustSlotCount(slots: InventorySlot[], slotId: string, delta: number, preferredIndex?: number): InventorySlot[] | null
```
Adds or removes items from a slot array. For dungeon loot, use with `delta > 0` to insert items into an empty chest.

```typescript
export function tryInsertSlots(inventory: InventorySlot[], incoming: InventorySlot[]): InventorySlot[] | null
```
**Most useful for dungeon loot.** All-or-nothing insertion of a pre-built slot array into a chest. Stackable items merge into existing stacks or empty slots; durability items (tools/weapons/armor) each take a whole slot to preserve individual durability values. Returns `null` if everything doesn't fit—caller leaves chest untouched.

Example usage:
```typescript
const lootSlots = [
  createSlot("diamond_sword", 1),      // durability preserved separately
  createSlot("gold_ore", 3),           // stackable
  createSlot("leather", 5),            // stackable material
  createEmptySlot(),
  // ... fill to CHEST_SLOTS with empty slots
];
const chestSlots = Array.from({ length: CHEST_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
const result = tryInsertSlots(chestSlots, lootSlots);
if (result) state.containers.set(chestIndex, result);
```

**Supporting Functions:**
- `createSlot(itemId: string, count: number): InventorySlot` — Creates a populated slot with correct label, kind, durability (for tools/armor)
- `createEmptySlot(): InventorySlot` — Returns `{ id: null, label: "Empty", kind: null, count: 0 }`
- `cloneSlots(slots: InventorySlot[]): InventorySlot[]` — Deep copy of slot array (all mutations return new arrays, never mutate input)

# Implementation Plan: Dungeons / Loot Structures (v0.7.0)

Caveman cadence for substance; full prose where step-order matters.

## 0. Verified facts (corrections to prompt)

- `SAVE_KEY` already `"minecraft_save_v5"` in `config.ts:90`. But `SaveData.version` still `4`, type comment says v4. So: bump is **version 4 -> 5** in the type/migration layer. `SAVE_KEY` string itself: see decision in §3 — worldgen change forces a NEW key (`minecraft_save_v6`) because old block-diffs index against the old baseline.
- Block-rendered inventory icons auto-generate via `paintIsoBlock()` (`lib/ui/spritePixels.ts:544`) from `BLOCK_COLORS` — no per-block grid needed for plain blocks. Good news for mossy cobble/spawner.
- New block typecheck-gated by `GROUP_BY_BLOCK` exhaustive record in `lib/game/audio/materials.ts:9` — MUST add entry or build fails.
- `spritePixels.test.ts` integrity test fails if item id falls to placeholder checker — block items pass via `paintIsoBlock`, so OK as long as `ITEM_DEFS` entry has `kind:"block"` + `blockId`.

---

## 1. Worldgen — `placeDungeons()`

### Design

New fn in `lib/world/generation.ts`, mirror `placeStructures`/`placeHouse`.

**Room shape**: hollow cobblestone box. Interior `5x4x5` (Minecraft dungeon = ~7x7 floor; we go small per "minimal slice"). Shell = cobble (+ optional mossy cobble speckle, §5). Hollow interior Air. Solid cobble floor. Chests 1-2 against a wall. Optional spawner block centered on floor.

**Count**: new GEN const `dungeonCount: 28` (houses=120 surface; dungeons fewer + underground). Tune later.

**Placement (deterministic, uses shared `rand()` stream)**:
```
for i in dungeonCount:
  cx = 12 + floor(rand()*(sizeX-24))
  cz = 12 + floor(rand()*(sizeZ-24))
  surface = highestSolidY(cx,cz)
  floorY = depthMin + floor(rand()*(depthSpan))   // underground band
  placeDungeon(world, cx, floorY, cz, rand)
```

Validation inside `placeDungeon` (return early if fail, like `placeHouse`):
- `floorY` band: between `y=8` and `surface-8` (underground, above bedrock-ish, below terrain). Reject if `floorY+roomH >= surface-3` (too shallow -> pokes surface) or `floorY < 6`.
- Avoid spawn area: reject if within radius (~40) of map center `(sizeX/2, sizeZ/2)` — player spawns there (`findSpawnOnLand` from center). Prevents free starter loot.
- Biome rules: none needed first slice. Dungeons underground, biome-agnostic. (Could skip Ocean later; not v0.7.0.)
- Bounds check all 6 walls `inBounds`.

**Carve+build order** (deterministic, idempotent over the cobble it writes):
1. Fill solid cobble box `[cx-half..cx+half] x [floorY-1..floorY+roomH] x [cz-half..cz+half]` (shell-fill-then-hollow simplest + robust vs surrounding caves/ore/stone).
2. Hollow interior to Air (inset 1 on all axes), but keep `floorY-1` as solid floor.
3. Place chests: pick 1-2 wall-adjacent floor cells deterministically (`rand()`). `world.set(...BlockId.Chest)`.
4. Optional spawner: `world.set(cx, floorY, cz, BlockId.Spawner)` (§4).

Carving cobble shell INTO surrounding caves is fine — that's the point (room reads as built room inside cave/stone).

### Integration order in `generateWorld`

```
generateTerrain → carveCaves → placeWater → placeBeaches → placeOres → placeTrees → placeCacti → placeStructures → placeDungeons
```
Append **last**. Rationale: dungeons overwrite whatever was there (cobble shell wins over ore/cave/stone), and they must not be eaten by later passes. Append after `placeStructures` so houses unaffected and dungeon `rand()` draws come last in the stream (minimizes diff to existing structure placement — though ANY new `rand()` call shifts nothing before it; appending keeps houses byte-identical-in-isolation but full-world hash still changes because dungeon writes change bytes).

### Deriving dungeon chest/spawner index sets on load

**Recommendation: re-run `placeDungeons` is NOT needed as a separate pass. Instead split placement into a pure index-collector.**

Refactor `placeDungeon` to optionally **record** the voxel indices it places chests/spawners at, into an out-array, instead of (or in addition to) writing blocks. Two call modes:

```ts
// generation.ts
export type DungeonSites = { chestIndices: number[]; spawnerIndices: number[] };

// build mode (worldgen): writes blocks
function placeDungeons(world, rand): void

// derive mode (load): re-runs SAME rand-consuming placement math but only
// collects indices, no block writes. Deterministic per seed.
export function collectDungeonSites(world: VoxelWorld): DungeonSites
```

Problem: `placeDungeons` consumes the shared `rand()` stream that's threaded through ALL prior passes. To re-derive on load WITHOUT regenerating the whole world, `collectDungeonSites` must reproduce the rand state at the point dungeons run. **Two clean options:**

**Option A (recommended): dedicated sub-seed.** Give dungeons their own LCG seeded from `world.seed`, NOT the shared stream:
```ts
function dungeonRand(seed: number) { /* mulberry32(seed ^ 0xD06... ) */ }
```
Then `collectDungeonSites(world)` re-creates that exact LCG from `world.seed` and replays placement math (reading `world.blocks` for validation — the world is already fully generated+block-diff-applied at load time, but validation reads must match generation-time reads).

⚠ **Validation-read hazard**: generation-time `placeDungeon` validates against the *partially-generated* world (post-cacti). Load-time the world is *fully generated then block-diffed*. If validation reads terrain that the dungeon itself or block-diffs changed, the two passes diverge -> wrong index set -> loot exploit returns. **Mitigation**: make validation read ONLY terrain that's stable between gen-end and load (`highestSolidY`, biome — both deterministic from seed, unaffected by dungeon writes if we never validate against cobble we placed). Validate using `getBiome`/`highestSolidY`/center-distance only — all pure-from-seed. Avoid reading block types that dungeon placement or player edits mutate. Keep `placeDungeon` validation **terrain-only**.

The collector at load re-runs `generateTerrain` cost? No — `highestSolidY` reads the live (regenerated) `world.blocks`, which IS regenerated each load (GameEngine ctor calls `generateWorld`). So at load, after `generateWorld`, the blocks include dungeon cobble. `highestSolidY(cx,cz)` returns surface (cobble is underground, doesn't change surface). Safe. Center-distance + biome + surfaceY all reproduce. 

**Cost**: `collectDungeonSites` = `dungeonCount`(28) iterations of cheap math + a few `highestSolidY` calls each. Negligible (microseconds). Run once in GameEngine ctor after `generateWorld`. No need to optimize.

**Option B (rejected): persist the index sets in the save.** Bloats save, redundant (derivable from seed), and breaks if seed/gen logic changes. Reject.

### Flags
- **Worldgen hash WILL change** -> re-baseline all 4 digests in `generation.test.ts` (3 small-world + 1 full + meshing geometry hash if region overlaps a dungeon — likely not, region is 0-127 surface; dungeon math may place one there. Check after.).
- **SAVE_KEY bump**: `minecraft_save_v5` -> `minecraft_save_v6`. World-reset accepted per prompt.

---

## 2. Loot system — `lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`

New module, mirror `mobLoot.ts` exactly (`MobDrop` shape, `clampUnit`, roll loop).

```ts
import type { MobDrop } from "@/lib/game/mobLoot"; // reuse type, or redefine local LootEntry

export type LootEntry = { itemId: string; min: number; max: number; chance?: number };
export type LootTier = "common" | "rare";

// Tiered: each chest rolls a tier, then rolls that tier's table.
export const DUNGEON_LOOT: Record<LootTier, LootEntry[]>;

export function rollDungeonLoot(rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }>;
```

### Tiers (given existing item set)

Items available: ores (`*_ore` blocks), gear (`*_pickaxe`, `*_sword`, armor pieces, `knife`), food (`bread`, `cooked_chicken/mutton`, `food`), materials (`wool/feather/bone/leather/string/seeds/wheat`), blocks.

**common** (most chests):
- `bread` 1-3 (chance .8)
- `cooked_chicken` 1-2 (chance .5)
- `bone` 0-3
- `string` 0-2
- `sliver_ore` / `gold_ore` 1-3 (chance .4 each)
- `seeds` 0-2
- `stone_pickaxe` 0-1 (chance .25)

**rare** (small chance, the payoff):
- `diamond_ore` 1-2 (chance .3)
- `sapphire_ore` 1-2 (chance .35)
- `ruby_sword` / `sapphire_sword` (chance .15 each, count 1)
- `chestplate` / `helmet` (chance .2 each, count 1)
- `diamond_pickaxe` (chance .08, count 1)
- plus a common sub-roll so rare chests also have staples.

`rollDungeonLoot`: roll tier (e.g. `rng() < 0.25 -> rare`), then iterate that table. Durable items (gear) roll count 1; `tryInsertSlots` gives each a fresh slot w/ full durability via `createSlot`.

### Deterministic per-chest seeding

Each chest fills lazily on first open (§3) using an rng seeded from the **chest's voxel index + world seed**:
```ts
const chestRng = mulberry32((world.seed ^ idx ^ 0x9E3779B1) >>> 0);
```
-> reproducible loot until opened. Same chest, same seed -> same loot every probe (testable). Put a small `mulberry32` helper in `dungeonLoot.ts` or reuse existing (engine test has one privately; add a shared `lib/game/rng.ts` if none exported — check: `generation.ts` inlines its own, `GameEngine.test` inlines `mulberry32`. Add tiny exported `seededRng(seed)` to `dungeonLoot.ts`).

Integrity test (config.test.ts pattern): every loot `itemId` ∈ `ITEM_DEFS`. Add to `dungeonLoot.test.ts`.

---

## 3. Loot persistence — solve infinite-loot exploit

### Recommended solution

**Session-derived dungeon-chest index Set (from seed) + persisted "looted" Set + lazy fill on first open.** This is the prompt's option C, and it's cleanest. Lifecycle:

```
unopened chest:  not in containers Map, idx ∈ dungeonChestIndices, idx ∉ lootedSet
first open:      interactChest sees dungeon chest + not looted -> roll loot -> fill 27-slot array -> mark looted
opened (looted): idx ∈ lootedSet -> interactChest skips roll, opens whatever's there (incl. empty)
reload:          lootedSet restored from save -> chest never re-rolls. If player emptied it,
                 serializeContainers skips it (empty), BUT lootedSet keeps it marked -> no re-roll. EXPLOIT CLOSED.
```

Key insight: **the persisted `lootedSet` is the source of truth for "has this been opened", decoupled from whether the container is currently non-empty.** Empty-container-skip in `serializeContainers` no longer causes re-roll, because the gate is `lootedSet`, not container presence.

### State additions (`state.ts`)

```ts
// GameState:
dungeonChestIndices: Set<number>;   // session, derived from collectDungeonSites at boot
dungeonSpawnerIndices: Set<number>; // session, derived (§4)
lootedDungeonChests: Set<number>;   // persisted -> save field
```

### Save format change (v5 -> additive, `types.ts`)

```ts
export type SaveData = Omit<SaveDataV4, "version"> & {
  version: 5;
  lootedChests?: number[];   // voxel indices of dungeon chests already opened
};
// rename current SaveData -> SaveDataV4, add version:4 literal kept as legacy type
```
Restructure: current `SaveData` (v4) becomes `SaveDataV4`; new `SaveData` is v5.

### Migration (`save.ts`)

```ts
export function migrateSaveV4toV5(save: SaveDataV4): SaveData {
  return { ...save, version: 5 }; // pure bump; lootedChests absent -> no looted chests
}
```
Chain in `readSave`: `...if (migrated.version === 4) migrated = migrateSaveV4toV5(migrated); if (migrated.version !== 5) return null;`

⚠ **BUT**: even though migration is *additive/pure*, the **worldgen change** (dungeons now generated) means old block-diffs index against an old baseline -> corruption. So per `docs/save-format.md` rule and `testing.md` policy: **bump `SAVE_KEY` to `minecraft_save_v6`**. Old saves silently dropped (accepted). The additive save *shape* migration still matters for forward-compat hygiene and tests, but the key bump is the real gate.

### serialize/read changes

`save.ts`:
```ts
export function serializeLootedChests(set: Set<number>): number[] { return [...set]; }
export function readLootedChests(save: SaveData): number[] {
  return Array.isArray(save.lootedChests) ? save.lootedChests.filter(Number.isFinite) : [];
}
```
`GameEngine.serialize()`: add `lootedChests: serializeLootedChests(state.lootedDungeonChests)`, set `version: 5`.
`GameEngine` ctor: after `generateWorld`, `state.dungeonChestIndices = new Set(sites.chestIndices)` etc; if save, `state.lootedDungeonChests = new Set(readLootedChests(save))`.

### interactChest modification (`interact.ts`)

```ts
function interactChest(state, emit, x, y, z): boolean {
  const idx = state.world.index(x, y, z);
  if (!state.containers.has(idx)) {
    const slots = Array.from({length: CHEST_SLOTS}, () => createEmptySlot());
    // Lazy dungeon-loot fill: only a worldgen dungeon chest, only first ever open.
    if (state.dungeonChestIndices.has(idx) && !state.lootedDungeonChests.has(idx)) {
      const rng = seededRng((state.world.seed ^ idx ^ 0x9e3779b1) >>> 0);
      for (const { itemId, count } of rollDungeonLoot(rng)) {
        // fill via tryInsertSlots into the chest array, or adjustSlotCount on slots
        const merged = tryInsertSlots(slots, [createSlot(itemId, count)]); // careful: tryInsertSlots takes inventory+incoming
        // simpler: insertSlots(slots, createSlot(...)) — reuse inventory.tryInsertSlots(slots, [slot])
      }
    }
    state.containers.set(idx, slots);
  }
  if (state.dungeonChestIndices.has(idx)) state.lootedDungeonChests.add(idx); // mark on open
  state.openContainerIndex = idx;
  state.inventoryOpen = true;
  emit({ type: "openedContainer" });
  return true;
}
```
Note: `tryInsertSlots(inventory, incoming)` is all-or-nothing into `inventory` array — pass the chest `slots` as the inventory arg, `[createSlot(itemId,count)]` as incoming. Accumulate into a working array. 27 slots >> loot count, always fits. Mark looted **on open** (not on empty) — that's the exploit fix.

`dungeonChestIndices`/`lootedDungeonChests` must be passed into `interactChest` via `state` (already have `state`). Add fields to `GameState`. ✓

Edge: player breaks a dungeon chest before opening. `spillChestOnBreak` (mining.ts) reads `state.containers.get(idx)` — empty (never filled) -> spills nothing, deletes entry. Loot lost (chest destroyed unopened). Acceptable + matches Minecraft (break empty-looking chest = nothing). Optionally fill-on-break too: in `mining.ts` before spill, if `dungeonChestIndices.has(idx) && !looted`, fill it. **Recommend: yes, fill-on-break too** so breaking a dungeon chest yields its loot. Add same fill helper. Mark looted there as well. Extract fill into shared `fillDungeonChestIfUnlooted(state, idx): InventorySlot[]` helper (interact.ts or new dungeon module) used by both interact + mining.

## 4. Spawner — **PHASE 2 within feature (optional, low risk, recommend include)**

Risk assessment: low. Mobs session-only, never saved. Spawner block tracked by session-derived Set (same as chests). No save complexity. Include it — it's the "dungeon" payoff vibe. Mark separable so it can be a later commit.

### New block `BlockId.Spawner = 30` (after MossyCobble, see §5 numbering)

Registration (§5 checklist). Dark cage look: `BLOCK_COLORS` near-black `[0.18,0.19,0.22]`, atlas special-paint a grid/cage pattern (add branch in `atlas.ts` like furnace mouth). Not minable to item ideally — but simplest: give it `BREAK_HARDNESS` high (~30), no `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` -> mining drops nothing (like Minecraft spawner). Skip `ITEM_DEFS`/recipe (not craftable). NOT in `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` so `paintIsoBlock` icon irrelevant (never in inventory). **But** `GROUP_BY_BLOCK` exhaustive -> MUST add `[BlockId.Spawner]: "stone"`.

### `tickSpawnerDirector` (`spawnDirector.ts`)

```ts
export function tickSpawnerDirector(state, dt, rng, surfaceYAt): void {
  state.timers.spawnerTimer += dt;
  if (state.timers.spawnerTimer < SPAWNER_INTERVAL_SECONDS) return;
  state.timers.spawnerTimer = 0;
  const livingHostiles = state.mobs.filter(m => m.hostile).length;
  if (livingHostiles >= HOSTILE_CAP) return; // share global cap

  for (const idx of state.dungeonSpawnerIndices) {
    const [sx, sy, sz] = indexToXYZ(state.world, idx);
    // only spawn if player near (perf + "drip when near")
    const dist = Math.hypot(state.player.position.x - sx, state.player.position.z - sz);
    if (dist > SPAWNER_ACTIVATION_RADIUS) continue;
    // only if spawner block still present (player could mine it... it's near-unbreakable)
    if (state.world.get(sx, sy, sz) !== BlockId.Spawner) continue;
    if (state.mobs.filter(m => m.hostile).length >= SPAWNER_LOCAL_CAP) continue;
    const kind = pick(["zombie","skeleton","spider"], rng);
    spawnMobGroup(state, { kind, hostile:true, count:1, centerX:sx, centerZ:sz, radius:3 }, rng, surfaceYAt);
    emit spawn juice event;
  }
}
```
⚠ `spawnMobGroup` uses `randomLandPointNear` -> `surfaceYAt` (surface spawn). Dungeon is underground -> mob would spawn on surface above, wrong. **Need underground-aware spawn**: add a variant that spawns at the spawner's own `y` (floor of room) within radius, checking 2 air blocks. Either:
- (a) add `randomDungeonPointNear` in `spawn.ts` that searches near `(sx,sy,sz)` for 2-air-above-solid in-room, or
- (b) simplest: spawn directly at `(sx+0.5, sy+halfHeight, sz+0.5)` + small jitter, skip surface logic.
Recommend (b) for first slice — spawner spawns mob standing on the room floor. Build a small inline mob-create (or extend `spawnMobGroup` with an optional explicit `y`).

### Step order (`GameEngine.step`)

Insert after `tickHostileSpawnDirector`, before `tickMobs`:
```
...tickRandomBlocks → tickHostileSpawnDirector → tickSpawnerDirector → tickMobs → tickBreeding
```
Add `surfaceYAt`+`rng` deps (already in scope). Pass `emit` for juice.

### Config tunables (`config.ts`)

```ts
export const SPAWNER_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 8;
export const SPAWNER_ACTIVATION_RADIUS = 16;
export const SPAWNER_LOCAL_CAP = 6;   // per-area, under global HOSTILE_CAP
```
Add `spawnerTimer: 0` to `GameTimers` + `createTimers()`.

### Spawn juice event (`state.ts` GameEvent union)

```ts
| { type: "mobSpawned"; kind: MobKind; x: number; y: number; z: number }
```
- Audio: route in `audioDirector.ts handleEvent` (a poof — reuse a soft synth).
- Particle: `GameRenderer.handleEvent` add `case "mobSpawned"` -> small dark smoke burst (mirror `mobDied` burst, darker color). Event already carries x/y/z.

Population-cap interaction: spawner respects global `HOSTILE_CAP` (shared `state.mobs` count) + own `SPAWNER_LOCAL_CAP`. Night `tickHostileSpawnDirector` unaffected (independent timer). Both feed same `state.mobs`, same cap check -> no overrun.

## 5. New blocks / flavor

### Mossy cobblestone — RECOMMEND YES (cheap, big flavor win, zero asset)

Dungeon shell speckled with mossy cobble = instant Minecraft-dungeon read. Deterministic speckle in `placeDungeon` (hash-gated like ore specks).

`BlockId.MossyCobblestone = 28`? — **Numbering caution**: `const enum`, values baked at compile. Adding mid-enum shifts nothing if appended. Current max = `Chest=27`. Append: `MossyCobblestone = 28`, `Spawner = 29`. (Atlas `ATLAS_BLOCK_COUNT` auto-derives from `BLOCK_COLORS` max key -> grows automatically.)

⚠ Existing block-diff saves store raw BlockId numbers. New ids only appended -> old ids unchanged. But SAVE_KEY bumping anyway -> moot.

### Full registration checklist (per `docs/adding-content.md`) — **MossyCobblestone**

1. `blocks.ts`: `BlockId.MossyCobblestone = 28`; `BLOCK_COLORS[28] = [0.34,0.42,0.3]` (greenish cobble); `HELD_BLOCK_COLORS[28] = 0x6a7a55`.
2. `items.ts`: `BREAK_HARDNESS[MossyCobblestone] = 5`.
3. `items.ts`: `ITEM_DEFS` `{ id:"mossy_cobble", label:"Mossy Cobble", kind:"block", blockId:MossyCobblestone }`; `BLOCK_TO_SLOT[MossyCobblestone]="mossy_cobble"`. (Mineable, collectible.)
4. Recipe: optional, skip (found-only, more special).
5. Solid/opaque -> no meshing/`isSolid` work.
6. `materials.ts`: `[BlockId.MossyCobblestone]: "stone"` (REQUIRED — exhaustive).
7. Atlas: auto-tile from `BLOCK_COLORS`; optionally add moss-speckle branch in `atlas.ts` drawTile (`if mossy && n>0.7 -> green tone`).
8. Inventory icon: auto via `paintIsoBlock`. `spritePixels.test.ts` passes (block kind).
9. `config.test.ts` integrity auto-covers the mapping.

### Spawner block registration — same checklist, differences:

- `BLOCK_COLORS[29]=[0.18,0.19,0.22]`, `HELD_BLOCK_COLORS` optional (never held).
- `BREAK_HARDNESS[Spawner]=30` (hard); **no** `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` (drops nothing); **no** `ITEM_DEFS` (uncraftable, ungettable). 
- `materials.ts`: `[Spawner]:"stone"` REQUIRED.
- Atlas: add cage/grid special-paint branch.
- ⚠ Not in `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` -> `config.test.ts` "every droppable block maps" untouched (it iterates `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` values; spawner absent = fine). No `spritePixels` requirement (never inventory item).
- Mining: spawner has hardness but no drop -> `addBlockDrop` rolls nothing (no `BLOCK_TO_SLOT`). Player CAN eventually break it -> stops spawns (block gone, `tickSpawnerDirector` checks `world.get===Spawner`). Acceptable.

## Risks (explicit)

1. **collectDungeonSites divergence from placeDungeons** = THE critical risk. If load-time validation reads differ from gen-time, index sets mismatch -> exploit returns OR chests think they're unlooted wrongly. **Mitigation**: validation reads pure-from-seed terrain only (`getBiome`, center-distance, `highestSolidY`); never validate against blocks the dungeon/player mutates. Add a test that `collectDungeonSites` indices == actual `BlockId.Chest`/`Spawner` positions in generated world (proves the two passes agree).
2. **Worldgen hash re-baseline** = breaks save compat (intended). SAVE_KEY bump mandatory. World-reset accepted.
3. **Meshing geometry hash** may shift if a dungeon intersects test region — verify, re-baseline if so.
4. **Underground mob spawn** — `spawnMobGroup`'s surface logic wrong underground; need explicit-y spawn (§4b). Risk of mobs spawning in solid/suffocating if room too small — spawn at room center floor, room is hollow, safe.
5. **Spawner perf** — iterating `dungeonSpawnerIndices` every interval is fine (28 dungeons, 8s interval, distance-gated). No per-frame cost.
6. **const enum append** — safe (no value shifts); SAVE_KEY bump covers any concern.

A **session-derived `Set<number>` of dungeon-chest voxel indices**, recomputed\n   from the seed each load (no save bloat).\n2.
