1	# Testing
2	
3	Two runners, three layers:
4	
5	- **`bun test`** — unit, integration, and component tests, colocated as `*.test.ts[x]` next to the code they cover. The shared setup (`tests/setup.ts`, preloaded via `bunfig.toml`) registers happy-dom so React component tests run under the same runner; the game engine itself needs no DOM (see [architecture.md](architecture.md)).
6	- **`bun run test:e2e`** — Playwright browser smoke tests in `e2e/*.e2e.ts` against the production build. The file suffix matters: `bun test` auto-collects `*.test.ts` AND `*.spec.ts`, so E2E files must use `.e2e.ts`.
7	
8	## What is covered
9	
10	| Area                 | File                                                                                                                                  | What it pins down                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
11	| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
12	| Worldgen determinism | `lib/world/generation.test.ts`                                                                                                        | SHA-256 digests of generated worlds per seed (the save-compat contract), plus structural probes and meshing snapshots                                                                                                                                                                                       |
13	| Voxel store          | `lib/world/voxelWorld.test.ts`                                                                                                        | get/set/bounds/solidity and the voxel index formula saves depend on                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
14	| Raycast & collision  | `lib/world/queries.test.ts`                                                                                                           | DDA hit/previous cells and hit distance, AABB collision edges, water non-solidity                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
15	| Items & recipes      | `lib/game/config.test.ts`                                                                                                             | Referential integrity: every recipe/drop/armor mapping points at a real item                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
16	| Inventory algebra    | `lib/game/inventory.test.ts`                                                                                                          | Stack math, durability, crafting (including the refuse-on-full behavior), armor equip rules                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
17	| Save format          | `lib/game/save.test.ts`                                                                                                               | Round-trips, legacy-shape parsing, corrupt-data rejection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
18	| Simulation           | `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.test.ts`                                                                                                  | Headless gameplay: boot, movement, hunger/regen gates, mining, commands (incl. pause/debug/camera-view/respawn), death/respawn, night spawning, save round-trips, gameplay event emission (block break/place, hurt, eat, jump/land, mob attack/hit)                                                         |
19	| Weather              | `lib/game/engine/systems/weather.test.ts`                                                                                             | Deterministic precipitation windows (seeded active/clear, mid-window intensity peak, ~rain fraction) and biome → rain/snow/clear; transient, never serialized                                                                                                                                               |
20	| Audio data           | `lib/game/audio/{materials,soundParams}.test.ts`                                                                                      | Every `BlockId` has a material group; every group/`MobKind` has well-formed ZZFX sound entries                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
21	| Audio schedulers     | `lib/game/audio/{footsteps,mobAmbience,musicBrain}.test.ts`                                                                           | Stride cadence and airborne reset; earshot gating, distance gain, look-relative pan, seeded call intervals; notes stay on scale, day/night/biome moods, deterministic under a seeded rng                                                                                                                    |
22	| Audio routing        | `lib/game/audio/audioDirector.test.ts`                                                                                                | With an injected fake `SynthBackend`: events → correct sound tables, staged mining ticks, footsteps while walking, pause ducking, mute/volume application                                                                                                                                                   |
23	| Audio settings       | `lib/game/audio/settings.test.ts`                                                                                                     | Round-trips, corrupt-data fallback, range clamping (own localStorage key, not the world save)                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
24	| Skin presets         | `lib/game/{playerSkins,skinSettings}.test.ts`                                                                                         | Preset integrity (six unique, valid colors, default-id fallback) and preference persistence (round-trip, corrupt/unknown-id fallback, swallowed write failures)                                                                                                                                             |
25	| UI sprites           | `lib/ui/*.test.ts`                                                                                                                    | Every item id renders a non-empty deterministic 16×16 sprite; HUD icon variants (full/half/container) differ; every skin preset renders a distinct bust portrait in its own palette                                                                                                                         |
26	| Minimap sampling     | `lib/game/render/minimapColors.test.ts`                                                                                               | Top-block detection (water visible), height shading, per-block colors                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
27	| Renderer logic       | `lib/game/render/{mobVisuals,heldItem,heldItemPose,extrudedSprite,itemModel,playerModel,playerPose,playerVisuals,cameraView}.test.ts` | Model lifecycle (create/reuse/remove/dispose), positioning, bob, item swaps, held-item swing/equip/bob poses, sprite-extrusion face culling and vertex colors, player-body gait/chop poses and visibility per camera mode, third-person camera placement (rear/front, clamped boom) — pure Three.js, no DOM |
28	| Particles & sky      | `lib/game/render/{particlePool,particleSystem,starField}.test.ts`                                                                     | Particle pool spawn/integrate/retire, drop-oldest at cap, dt clamp, determinism; hex→RGB channel split; deterministic on-sphere star positions                                                                                                                                                              |
29	| Components           | `components/game/*.test.tsx`                                                                                                          | InventoryPanel click-to-swap/equip/craft gating, Hotbar slots, StatusBars heart/hunger/armor meters, PauseMenu, DeathScreen (happy-dom + Testing Library)                                                                                                                                                   |
30	| Browser E2E          | `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts`                                                                                                                    | Real browser: boot without console errors, scene draws, input → movement, craft via UI, mining, camera view cycling, skin pick persisting across reload, pause freeze/resume, save via pause menu across reload                                                                                             |
31	
32	## Running the E2E suite
33	
34	```bash
35	bunx playwright install chromium   # once
36	bun run test:e2e                   # builds and starts the prod server itself
37	```
38	
39	E2E tests assert through `window.__monecraft` (the live engine/renderer/input handle — also handy in the browser console) instead of pixels; the one render check uses `renderer.renderedTriangles()`. The shared fixture fails any test that logs a console error.
40	
41	**happy-dom has no 2D canvas context**: generated sprites degrade to a transparent data URL in component tests — assert sprite pixels in the pure `lib/ui` tests and component structure via roles/labels/`data-icon` attributes, not image sources.
42	
43	**happy-dom has no `AudioContext`** (and the `zzfx` package instantiates one at import): everything above the `SynthBackend` seam is pure and tested with a recording fake (see `audioDirector.test.ts`); the WebAudio glue itself (`synth.ts`, `musicPlayer.ts`, `rainLoop.ts`) plus real synthesis quality belong to the manual gameplay pass.
44	
45	**Known limitation:** headless Chromium cannot engage pointer lock (`requestPointerLock` never resolves). `acquirePointerLock` in `e2e/helpers.ts` tries the real thing, then falls back to forcing the input controller's lock flag — so key/mouse → engine wiring is still tested end to end, but real lock acquisition and look-around feel remain in the manual gameplay pass, along with visual quality. That manual pass is now only required for changes to pointer-lock handling or visual appearance.
46	
47	## The worldgen hash policy
48	
49	Saves store only the world seed plus block-change deltas, so `generateWorld` must produce byte-identical output for a given seed forever. The digests in `generation.test.ts` enforce that.
50	
51	**If a hash test fails after your change, your change breaks every existing save. Fix the code, never the hash.**
52	
53	Re-baselining the digests is legitimate in exactly two cases:
54	
55	1. A **deliberate worldgen change** — flag it in the PR and CHANGELOG, and bump `SAVE_KEY` so old saves are discarded rather than corrupted.
56	2. A **Bun version bump** — the noise functions use `Math.sin`, whose exact results are engine-defined (ECMA-262 leaves transcendentals implementation-specific). CI pins `bun-version` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` for this reason; bump both together and verify the digests merely shifted rather than the generation logic changing.
57	
58	To regenerate digests: temporarily log the computed hash in the failing test, run `bun test`, and paste the new values in the same commit as the cause.
59	
60	The same caveat means the hash tests prove _refactor purity on the pinned engine_, not cross-browser save portability — a player switching browsers may theoretically regenerate slightly different terrain for the same seed. That limitation predates the test suite; it is now at least documented.
61	
62	## Writing new tests
63	
64	- Pure logic (inventory, spawn rules, a new system): unit-test the module directly.
65	- Gameplay behavior: boot a small engine — `new GameEngine({ seed, rng, worldSize: { x: 64, y: 150, z: 64 } })` — and step it with synthetic `FrameInput`. Inject a seeded `rng` (see `mulberry32` in the engine tests) for determinism.
66	- The world boots at dawn with hostiles nearby; use the `calmDaytime` pattern from the engine tests when testing unrelated mechanics.
67	- A ray origin exactly on a voxel boundary may target a diagonal neighbor (DDA ambiguity) — center positions in cells when aiming at specific blocks.
68	
```

18:## React shell (`lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, `components/`)
30:## Game engine (`lib/game/engine/`)
38:### Per-frame step order (in `GameEngine.step`)
55:**Block-entities (chest contents).** Blocks are bare `BlockId`s, but a chest needs attached storage, so `state.containers: Map<voxelIndex, InventorySlot[]>` holds each placed chest's slots (keyed by `world.index`, the same space as the block diff). Placing a chest creates an empty entry; opening one (`interactChest`) sets `state.openContainerIndex` and `inventoryOpen` so the panel renders its grid; the `moveStack` command shuttles slots across the inventory/chest boundary (chest indices offset by `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE`); breaking a chest spills its contents into the inventory via `inventory.tryInsertSlots` (refused if they don't fit). `serialize()` persists non-empty containers as `blockEntities`; see [save-format.md](save-format.md).
57:## Renderer (`lib/game/render/`)

38	### Per-frame step order (in `GameEngine.step`)
39	
40	0. Pause gate: while `state.paused`, `step` refreshes the snapshot and returns — mobs, the day clock, mining, and stats all freeze (autosave still serializes fine)
41	1. Stuck detection / auto-unstuck (`STUCK_RESET_SECONDS`)
42	2. Death check + respawn countdown (while dead, only mobs tick)
43	3. Sleep gate: while `state.sleepTimer > 0`, `step` decrements the fade and returns (full freeze, like pause); at zero it skips the clock to the next morning
44	4. Player movement & physics (`systems/playerMotion.ts` — derives direction from `yaw`, scratch vectors, no per-frame allocations)
45	5. Hunger drain from sprint/walk/jump budgets + health regen (`systems/playerStats.ts`)
46	6. Mining progress and block breaking (`systems/mining.ts`; placement also lives here)
47	7. Day-night clock (`systems/dayNight.ts` — `daylightAt()` is the single daylight formula); immediately after, `systems/weather.ts` sets the **transient** `state.weather` (rain/snow/clear) as a pure function of `dayClock` + seed — cosmetic only: never serialized, never touches spawn/daylight balance
48	8. Random block ticks (`systems/randomTicks.ts` — each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers; drives crop growth and is the extension point for future saplings / grass spread)
49	9. Night hostile spawning (`systems/spawnDirector.ts`, interval/cap in config)
50	10. Mob AI: wander/aggro/flee, attacks with line-of-sight, daylight burn (`systems/mobAI.ts`)
51	11. Animal breeding (`systems/breeding.ts` — feed-to-love timers, baby maturity, pairing in-love adults within range; the passive cap bounds the population)
52	
53	Combat (`systems/combat.ts`) runs on the `attack` command rather than per frame. The `placeBlock` command runs a fixed right-click precedence (`systems/interact.ts`) — feed an aimed animal (`tryFeedAimedMob`) → interact with the aimed block (`tryInteractBlock`, e.g. sleep in a bed, open a furnace, open a chest) → use the held item (`tryUseHeldItem`, e.g. a hoe tills soil, seeds plant a crop) → `placeSelectedBlock` — so each interaction consumes the click instead of getting a block placed against it. New mechanics get a new system module and a slot in this sequence — don't grow the engine class with inline logic.
54	
55	**Block-entities (chest contents).** Blocks are bare `BlockId`s, but a chest needs attached storage, so `state.containers: Map<voxelIndex, InventorySlot[]>` holds each placed chest's slots (keyed by `world.index`, the same space as the block diff). Placing a chest creates an empty entry; opening one (`interactChest`) sets `state.openContainerIndex` and `inventoryOpen` so the panel renders its grid; the `moveStack` command shuttles slots across the inventory/chest boundary (chest indices offset by `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE`); breaking a chest spills its contents into the inventory via `inventory.tryInsertSlots` (refused if they don't fit). `serialize()` persists non-empty containers as `blockEntities`; see [save-format.md](save-format.md).
56	
57	## Renderer (`lib/game/render/`)
```

  "old_string": "**Block-entities (chest contents).** Blocks are bare `BlockId`s, but a chest needs attached storage, so `state.containers: Map<voxelIndex, InventorySlot[]>` holds each placed chest's slots (keyed by `world.index`, the same space as the block diff). Placing a chest creates an empty entry; opening one (`interactChest`) sets `state.openContainerIndex` and `inventoryOpen` so the panel renders its grid; the `moveStack` command shuttles slots across the inventory/chest boundary (chest indices offset by `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE`); breaking a chest spills its contents into the inventory via `inventory.tryInsertSlots` (refused if they don't fit). `serialize()` persists non-empty containers as `blockEntities`; see [save-format.md](save-format.md).",
  "new_string": "**Block-entities (chest contents).** Blocks are bare `BlockId`s, but a chest needs attached storage, so `state.containers: Map<voxelIndex, InventorySlot[]>` holds each placed chest's slots (keyed by `world.index`, the same space as the block diff). Placing a chest creates an empty entry; opening one (`interactChest`) sets `state.openContainerIndex` and `inventoryOpen` so the panel renders its grid; the `moveStack` command shuttles slots across the inventory/chest boundary (chest indices offset by `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE`); breaking a chest spills its contents into the inventory via `inventory.tryInsertSlots` (refused if they don't fit). `serialize()` persists non-empty containers as `blockEntities`; see [save-format.md](save-format.md).\n\n**Dungeon loot chests.** Dungeon chests are placed by worldgen, so their contents can't ride the block-diff baseline. The engine instead keeps two session-only sets — `dungeonChestIndices` / `dungeonSpawnerIndices` — rebuilt each boot from the seed via `collectDungeonSites` (`lib/world/generation.ts`, which replays the dungeon placement math without writing blocks), plus a **persisted** `lootedDungeonChests` set. On a dungeon chest's first open or break, `systems/dungeon.ts` `fillDungeonChestIfUnlooted` rolls loot seeded from `world.seed ^ voxelIndex` (`lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`) and marks the index looted. Gating on the looted set — not on whether the chest is currently non-empty — is what stops an emptied-then-reloaded chest from re-rolling (an empty container is dropped from `blockEntities`)."
}

47	`DAY_CYCLE_SECONDS` (240) sets the whole rhythm — a shorter day means more frequent
48	nights and more pressure. The three daylight thresholds are a **documented engine
49	invariant** (daylight ranges 0.04–1.0; see architecture.md): hostiles spawn below
50	0.28, spiders turn hostile below 0.42, and exposed hostiles burn above 0.72. Keep
51	them ordered `spawn ≤ spider_aggro` and `burn` well above both, or mobs will spawn
52	into instant sunlight. `HOSTILE_CAP` × `HOSTILE_SPAWN_INTERVAL_SECONDS` bounds how
53	crowded a night gets. Tests aim daylight explicitly (a `calmDaytime` helper) to
54	avoid first-night aggro.

```
127	`RENDER_GRID`, `THIRD_PERSON_DISTANCE`, `THIRD_PERSON_MARGIN`.
128	
129	`RENDER_RADIUS` is the biggest **performance** lever: the renderer meshes one region
130	of this radius around the player, so larger values draw more terrain at higher cost;
131	`RENDER_GRID` is how far the player moves before that mesh rebuilds (smaller = more
132	frequent rebuilds, fresher view). `STUCK_RESET_SECONDS` is how long an overlap is
133	tolerated before the auto-unstuck teleport fires.
134	
135	## Save- and worldgen-sensitive tunables
136	
137	Change these only with care:
138	
139	- **`SAVE_KEY`** (`"minecraft_save_v5"`) is the localStorage key name. Note it's
140	  versioned independently of the save **schema** (currently v4, per
141	  [save-format.md](save-format.md)) — renaming `SAVE_KEY` orphans every existing
142	  player's save. Don't bump it to express a schema change; add a migration instead.
143	- **`INVENTORY_SLOTS` / `HOTBAR_SLOTS` / `MAX_STACK_SIZE`** affect the saved
144	  inventory layout. `save.ts` already migrates the v1 (40-slot) → v2 (36-slot)
145	  change; altering these again needs a matching migration or old saves break.
146	- **Daylight thresholds and `WAKE_DAY_PHASE`** shape the persisted `dayClock`'s
147	  meaning but are not save-format-breaking on their own.
148	- **Worldgen is not tuned here.** Terrain/ore/structure constants live in the
149	  frozen `GEN` object in `generation.ts` and are a byte-identical save contract
150	  pinned by hash tests — changing them requires the re-baseline policy in
151	  [testing.md](testing.md).
```

```
49	## An interactive block (right-click behavior)
50	
51	- Right-click (and KeyE) dispatch `placeBlock`, which runs a fixed precedence in `GameEngine.dispatch` before falling through to placement: feed an aimed mob → `tryInteractBlock` → use the held item → place. To make a block do something on right-click, register it in `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` and add a branch in `tryInteractBlock` (`lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts`).
52	- The handler returns `true` to consume the click (no block is placed) — return `true` even when the action is refused (e.g. a bed during the day) so the player doesn't place a block into the bed by accident.
53	- Three reference implementations: the **bed** sets `state.spawnPoint` and starts the sleep fade (`state.sleepTimer`); the **furnace** opens the inventory and sets `state.craftingStation`, which unlocks its `station` recipes (see "A new item or recipe"); the **chest** opens the inventory and sets `state.openContainerIndex` (see below). A block that opens the inventory from a mouse click also needs an `openedStation`/`openedContainer` handler in `useMinecraftGame` to release pointer lock. See [architecture.md](architecture.md) for the step order.
54	
55	## A container block (block-entity storage)
56	
57	- Blocks are bare `BlockId`s, so any block that needs **attached data** (a chest's items) stores it in `state.containers: Map<voxelIndex, InventorySlot[]>`, keyed by `world.index(x,y,z)`. The chest is the reference:
58	  - **Open**: `interactChest` (an `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` branch) lazily creates the slot array, sets `state.openContainerIndex`, opens the inventory, and emits `openedContainer`. The snapshot exposes `container`, and `InventoryPanel` renders a second grid; `toggleInventory`/`pause` clear `openContainerIndex`.
59	  - **Move items**: the `moveStack` command swaps a slot across the inventory/chest boundary using the pure `inventory.moveStack` (chest indices offset by `CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE` in `commands.ts`).
60	  - **Place / break**: placing the block (`mining.placeSelectedBlock`) seeds an empty container; breaking it spills the contents into the inventory via `inventory.tryInsertSlots` (all-or-nothing — a break is refused with a `breakBlocked` event when they don't fit).
61	  - **Persist**: `serialize()` writes non-empty containers to `blockEntities` (`save.serializeContainers`); boot restores them (`save.readContainers`) only for indices that still hold the block. This is an **additive save bump** — see [save-format.md](save-format.md).
62	
63	## A random-tick behavior (growth / spread)
64	
65	- Block updates that happen "over time" run through `lib/game/engine/systems/randomTicks.ts`: every `RANDOM_TICK_INTERVAL_SECONDS` it samples `RANDOM_TICK_SAMPLES` columns within `RANDOM_TICK_RADIUS` of the player and runs a handler on each column's top block. Register a `BlockId → handler` in `RANDOM_TICK_HANDLERS`; the handler edits via `state.blockChanges` and sets `state.worldMeshDirty`.
66	- The crop handler is the reference: wheat stage ids are consecutive, so growth is `block + 1`, and the mature stage has no handler so it stops. Because crops are ordinary block edits, they persist for free via the save's block diff — no new save fields.
67	- Tunables live in `config.ts`; the headless test pattern (a minimal `GameState`, a scripted rng that maps a sample onto a known column) is in `randomTicks.test.ts`.
68	
69	## A new mechanic
70	
71	Add a system module under `lib/game/engine/systems/` (a function over `GameState`), give it a slot in the `GameEngine.step` sequence, and put its tunables in `lib/game/config.ts`. If the UI triggers it, add a `Command` variant. Write its headless test next to it.
72	
```

If the UI triggers it, add a `Command` variant. Write its headless test next to it.",
  "new_string": "## A worldgen structure (houses, dungeons, …)\n\n- Structures are placed in `lib/world/generation.ts` at the end of the `generateWorld` pipeline. Mirror `placeHouse`/`placeDungeons`: loop a `GEN.<thing>Count`, sample positions, validate, and write blocks with `world.set`. Append your pass **last** so later passes don't overwrite it, and add the count to the frozen `GEN` object.\n- **Any block write changes the deterministic output**, so this breaks the worldgen hash. Re-baseline the `generation.test.ts` digests and bump `SAVE_KEY` per the [testing.md](testing.md) policy; add a structural probe test (count your blocks, assert they generate) that survives the re-baseline.\n- If the structure needs its positions known at runtime (dungeons re-derive chest/spawner indices on load), expose a **derive pass** like `collectDungeonSites`: factor placement into a shared routine that either writes blocks or records indices, drawing from a **dedicated PRNG seeded only from `world.seed`** (not the shared gen stream) with a fixed number of draws per structure, and validate against **seed-pure** terrain (`terrainTopY`, `getBiome`) — never `highestSolidY`, which a cave or player edit can shift out from under the derive pass.\n\n## A loot table / lazy block-entity fill\n\n- Drop tables are pure data + a roll function: copy the `{ itemId, min, max, chance? }` shape and the `clampUnit` roll loop from `lib/game/mobLoot.ts` / `lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`. Add a `*.test.ts` asserting every `itemId` exists in `ITEM_DEFS` and that rolls are deterministic under an injected rng.\n- To fill a **worldgen-placed** block-entity (a dungeon chest) whose contents can't live in the block-diff baseline: fill it lazily on first access, seeded from `world.seed ^ voxelIndex` (`dungeonLoot.seededRng`), and persist a set of *accessed* indices (additive save field) — gate the fill on that set, **not** on whether the container is currently non-empty, or an emptied-then-reloaded entity re-rolls. `lib/game/engine/systems/dungeon.ts` (`fillDungeonChestIfUnlooted`), shared by the open and break paths, is the reference; see the exploit-guard test in `GameEngine.test.ts`.\n\n## A new mechanic\n\nAdd a system module under `lib/game/engine/systems/` (a function over `GameState`), give it a slot in the `GameEngine.step` sequence, and put its tunables in `lib/game/config.ts`. If the UI triggers it, add a `Command` variant. Write its headless test next to it."
}

```
119	## Mining and building
120	
121	**Break** a block by holding left-click; a crack overlay shows progress, and harder
122	blocks take longer. **Place** the selected hotbar item with right-click (or `E`)
123	against a surface. Your reach is about **7 blocks**.
124	
125	**Tools gate what you can mine.** A pickaxe's tier determines which blocks it can
126	harvest at all — mining with too weak a tool yields nothing:
127	
128	| To mine…                  | You need at least… |
129	| ------------------------- | ------------------ |
130	| Stone, cobblestone, brick | Wood pickaxe       |
131	| Sliver ore                | Stone pickaxe      |
132	| Ruby & gold ore           | Sliver pickaxe     |
133	| Sapphire & diamond ore    | Ruby pickaxe       |
134	
135	Higher-tier pickaxes (sapphire, gold, diamond) also mine **faster**. Dirt, sand,
136	wood, leaves, and crops need no special tool. Ores hide underground and in caves;
137	the rarer the ore, the deeper it tends to sit.
138	
139	## Crafting
140	
141	Open the inventory (`I`) to reach the crafting panel. Pick a recipe whose
142	ingredients you have and craft it — the result drops into your inventory (crafting
143	is refused if there's no room rather than destroying the overflow). Stacks hold up
144	to **99** items; tools, weapons, and armor take one slot each and show durability.
145	
146	The basic recipe grid is always available. **Smelting** recipes (cooking raw meat)
147	require a **furnace**: craft one from 8 cobble, place it, and right-click it to open
148	the panel in furnace mode — the cooking recipes unlock while a furnace is open and
149	show as "Requires Furnace" otherwise.
150	
151	See the full list of **32 recipes** in the [reference](reference.md#recipes).
152	
153	## Storage
154	
155	Your pack only holds 36 slots, so build **chests** to stash the overflow. Craft a
156	chest from **8 planks**, place it, and **right-click** it to open it: a **27-slot**
157	grid appears above your inventory. Move items the same way you rearrange the
158	inventory — **click a slot, then click where it should go** — across either grid.
```

  "new_string": "Higher-tier pickaxes (sapphire, gold, diamond) also mine **faster**. Dirt, sand,\nwood, leaves, and crops need no special tool. Ores hide underground and in caves;\nthe rarer the ore, the deeper it tends to sit.\n\n## Dungeons and loot\n\nDigging around underground can turn up a **dungeon**: a small cobblestone room,\nflecked with mossy cobble, sealed in the rock away from your spawn. Inside are\n**loot chests** — open them for a free haul of food, ores, and sometimes real\ngear (a sword, armor, even a diamond pickaxe if you're lucky). Each chest's loot\nis fixed until you reach it, and once you've opened (or broken) it, it won't\nrefill — so there's no farming the same chest twice.\n\nThe catch: most dungeons hold a **spawner**, a dark caged block that conjures a\nhostile every few seconds as long as you're standing near it. They keep coming\nuntil you back off — or **mine the spawner block out**, which shuts it down for\ngood (it's tough and drops nothing). Grab the loot, deal with the mob, and leave,\nor clear the spawner first if you want to loot in peace.\n\n## Crafting"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/CHANGELOG.md",
  "old_string": "## [Unreleased]\n\n## [0.6.0] - 2026-06-13",
  "new_string": "## [Unreleased]\n\n## [0.7.0] - 2026-06-14\n\n### Added\n\n- **Dungeons & loot**: digging underground now turns up small cobblestone rooms (speckled with mossy cobble) sealed in the rock, well clear of spawn — the game's first real exploration payoff, and a found-loot purpose for chests. All procedural, **zero new assets**\n  - Each dungeon holds 1–2 **pre-filled loot chests** and a central **mob spawner**. Loot is tiered (`lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`): common chests carry food, low ores, and the occasional stone pickaxe (bone always drops, so a chest is never empty); a 25% rare roll adds the payoff — diamond/sapphire ore, a ruby/sapphire sword, helmet/chestplate, or a rare diamond pickaxe\n  - **Spawners** drip a hostile (zombie/skeleton/spider) onto the room floor every ~8 s while you're within ~16 blocks, up to 6 clustered nearby (under the global hostile cap). They're time-independent (dungeons are dark); **mining the spawner block out** stops it for good (it's hard and drops nothing). New `mobSpawned` event drives a synthesized low whoosh and a dark conjuring smoke puff\n  - New **MossyCobblestone** (mineable into a `mossy_cobble` item, found-only) and **Spawner** blocks, both with procedurally-painted atlas tiles\n  - **The lazy-loot design** solves an infinite-loot trap: dungeon chests are placed by deterministic worldgen, so their contents can't live in the block-diff save. Filling them eagerly would let a player empty a chest, reload (the empty chest is dropped from the save), and find fresh loot. Instead the engine fills each chest lazily on **first open or break** — loot seeded from `world.seed ^ voxelIndex` so it's reproducible until you reach it — and records the index in a persisted `lootedChests` set. Gating on \"has this been accessed\", not \"is it currently empty\", closes the re-roll. The set of *which* indices are dungeon chests/spawners is session-only, re-derived from the seed each load via `collectDungeonSites` (which replays the placement math against a dedicated seed-only PRNG and seed-pure terrain estimates, never `highestSolidY`, so it can't diverge from generation)\n  - **Save format v5** (additive `lootedChests` field; `migrateSaveV4toV5` is a pure version bump) and **`SAVE_KEY` bumped `minecraft_save_v5` → `minecraft_save_v6`**: the dungeon worldgen changes the deterministic terrain baseline, so **existing saved worlds are discarded** (a deliberate, documented re-baseline — worldgen SHA-256 digests and the in-region meshing snapshot were re-rolled; the structural probes confirm the surface is untouched since dungeons are wholly underground)\n\n## [0.6.0] - 2026-06-13"
}
```

```
! gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main --head feat/dungeons --title "Dungeons & loot structures (0.7.0)" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## What

Underground **dungeons** — the game's first real exploration payoff. Digging around turns up small cobblestone rooms (speckled with mossy cobble), sealed in the rock and kept clear of spawn, each with 1–2 **pre-filled tiered loot chests** and a central **mob spawner**.

- **Loot** (`lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`): common chests carry food / low ores / an occasional stone pickaxe (bone always drops, so never empty); a 25% rare roll adds diamond/sapphire ore, a ruby/sapphire sword, helmet/chestplate, or a rare diamond pickaxe.
- **Spawners** drip a hostile onto the room floor every ~8s while you're within ~16 blocks, up to 6 nearby (under the global cap); time-independent, and mining the block out stops it. New `mobSpawned` event drives a synth whoosh + dark smoke puff.
- New **MossyCobblestone** and **Spawner** blocks — procedural tiles, zero new assets.

## The tricky bit: no infinite loot

Dungeon chests are placed by deterministic worldgen, so their contents can't live in the block-diff save. Filling them eagerly would let you empty a chest, reload (the empty chest drops from the save), and find fresh loot. Instead chests fill **lazily on first open/break** — loot seeded from `world.seed ^ voxelIndex`, reproducible until reached — and the engine persists a `lootedChests` set. Gating on "accessed", not "currently empty", closes the re-roll. The set of *which* indices are dungeon sites is re-derived from the seed each load via `collectDungeonSites` (dedicated seed-only PRNG + seed-pure terrain validation, so it can't diverge from generation).

## Save / worldgen impact

- **Save format v5** (additive `lootedChests`; pure version-bump migration).
- **`SAVE_KEY` v5 → v6** — the dungeon worldgen changes the deterministic terrain baseline, so **existing worlds are discarded** (a deliberate, documented re-baseline; worldgen + meshing digests re-rolled, structural probes confirm the surface is untouched since dungeons are underground).

## Verification

`bun run lint` / `typecheck` / `format:check` / `bun test` (371 pass) / `bun run build` all green; `bun run test:e2e` 10/10. New tests cover the worldgen dungeon presence + `collectDungeonSites` agreement, loot determinism, the v4→v5 migration, the reload-after-emptying **exploit guard**, and spawner drip/cap/range/mined-out behavior.
EOF
)"
```

16	Still open, in rough order of how strongly they now build on what exists:
17	
18	- **Dungeons / exploration & loot** ⭐ (agreed next) — a worldgen structure pass placing rooms with **pre-filled loot chests** (reuses the just-built container/block-entity layer + the `placeStructures` hook in `generation.ts` + the `rollMobDrops` loot pattern) and optionally a mob spawner. Fills the "no exploration payoff" gap and gives chests a reason to exist beyond storage. Minimal first slice: a few small underground cobble rooms with 1–2 tiered loot chests each, optional drip-spawner. Cost: worldgen change → **`SAVE_KEY` bump + worldgen hash re-baseline** (documented policy in docs/testing.md). Medium–high effort.
19	- **Caves & survival tension** — torches + a real block-light propagation system (caves are currently lit like the surface, no darkness), plus lava + lava/drowning damage + an oxygen meter. Largest/riskiest: touches world storage (a per-voxel light channel) and the mesher hot path; est. ~1,500–2,200 LoC, multi-week.
20	- **Endgame & ranged combat** — bows/arrows as a transient projectile system (skeletons shoot back) + a boss encounter that makes the diamond grind a goal. Smallest/lowest-risk (~550–700 LoC, a few days), self-contained, **no save/worldgen impact**.
21	
22	Other gaps still noted: no block light, no fluid hazard, no ranged combat, no endgame target. The game already has caves, ores, ~120 houses, 5 (cosmetic) biomes, static water, 6 mobs, farming/breeding/cooking/beds, 7-tier gear, and now chests. ## Shipped: Dungeons & loot structures (0.7.0)

**PR open → https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/pull/17** (branch `feat/dungeons`, pushed)

**What's in the game now:** underground cobblestone rooms (mossy-flecked, kept clear of spawn), each with 1–2 tiered loot chests and a mob spawner that drips hostiles when you're near and stops when you mine it out. The game's first real exploration payoff — and a found-loot reason for chests to exist. All procedural, zero new assets.

**The hard part, solved:** dungeon chests are placed by deterministic worldgen, so naively pre-filling them would re-roll fresh loot every reload (infinite loot). They now fill lazily on first open/break, seeded by `world.seed ^ voxelIndex`, gated by a persisted "accessed" set — so emptying and reloading never re-rolls. The exploit-guard test pins exactly that.

**6 clean, bisectable commits** (blocks → loot table → worldgen → save/persistence → spawners → docs), with the only hash-touching change isolated to the worldgen commit.

**Verification — all green:**
- `bun run lint` (0 errors), `typecheck`, `format:check`, `bun run build`
- `bun test` — **371 pass**
- `bun run test:e2e` — **10/10** (it caught a stale save-version assertion mid-build, which I fixed)

**Heads-up — this resets worlds:** `SAVE_KEY` bumped v5→v6 because the dungeon worldgen changes the deterministic terrain baseline (you approved this). Existing saved worlds are discarded; worldgen + meshing hashes were re-baselined per the documented policy, with structural probes confirming the surface is untouched.

Docs updated across all 8 pages + CHANGELOG, and `package.json` is at 0.7.0. I also updated the roadmap memory to mark dungeons as built (PR #17) — caves/lighting and endgame/ranged remain as the open candidates.

> AGENT

Confirmed: **7 rare + 8 common = 15 entries max**, each producing at most one slot (counts are tiny — max 3, nowhere near the 99 stack limit; durables are count-1). A maximal roll is ≤15 slots, well under CHEST_SLOTS (27), so `tryInsertSlots` can never return null here. **The flagged loot loss is unreachable today** — it's a false positive for actual loss.

But the reviewer's underlying point is fair: `?? slots` silently swallows overflow, so it's a latent footgun if the tables ever grow past 27. The right fix isn't logging machinery for an unreachable case — it's to **lock the invariant with a test** (so CI fails if the tables ever outgrow a chest) plus a clarifying comment. Let me do that.

> AGENT

Here's the review status on **PR #17**:

## CodeRabbit review: 1 finding

**`lib/game/engine/systems/dungeon.ts:25` (marked Critical)** — `tryInsertSlots(slots, incoming) ?? slots` could silently discard loot if a roll exceeds the chest's 27 slots, while still marking the chest looted.

**My verdict: false positive for actual loot loss, but a fair latent-footgun point.** The loot table has only **15 entries total** (8 common + 7 rare), each producing at most one slot (counts max out at 3 — nowhere near the 99 stack limit — and gear is one-per-slot). A maximal roll is ≤15 slots, comfortably under 27, so `tryInsertSlots` can never return null today. The `?? slots` fallback is unreachable.

The legitimate part of the concern: if someone later grows the tables past 27, that fallback would swallow loot invisibly. So rather than add logging for an unreachable case, I **locked the invariant with a test**:
- asserts `common + rare entry count ≤ CHEST_SLOTS` (CI fails the day the tables outgrow a chest), plus
- a 500-roll fuzz confirming every real roll fits,
- and a comment at the call site explaining why the fallback is unreachable.

**Committed locally** as `211449e` (test + comment, no behavior change) — **not pushed**, per your "I check before push" workflow. Gate is green: typecheck, format, lint (0 errors), and the dungeonLoot suite (7 pass).

Nothing else was flagged — that was CodeRabbit's only actionable comment across all 33 files.

Want me to push `211449e` to update the PR? I can also reply to the CodeRabbit thread explaining the invariant if you'd like the resolution on the record.
